FOREWORD 

*Now go, write it before them in a table (Hab.2:2; 

Zech.5:2), and note it in a book (Rev. 10:2; 11:3), that it may be for the Time (of the end, Dan.12) to come for ever and ever: that this is a rebellious people, lying children; children that will not hear the law of the Lord: which say to the seers, See not; and to the prophets, Prophesy not unto us right things, speak unto us smooth things, prophesy deceits. Get you out of the way, turn aside out of the path, cause the HOLY ONE of Is-real to cease from before us.-Isa.30:8-11.

To WHOM is this command of God addressed, by the mouth of the prophet Isaiah? The reader now has "in hand" a chart and a book faithfully answering the above description . . . in which are defined the Signs-of-thetimes or the seven measures of the HOLY ONE, the degrees of soul-culture or the mystic-values of sacrednumbers. The burden of these, like a map, is to enable every progressive Christian to locate himself in the kingdom of God. The prophet Zechariah describes them as "antichrist (imperfection) detectors" or the Flying Roll and Ephah; the apostle John visualizes them, in his 

Apocalypse, as the visibilized or symbolical Two Witnesses of imputed and imparted righteousness. 

The prophet Daniel's Time-of-the-end going to and fro-COMPARISONS-and increase of knowledge, no doubt implies a consequent-interest in Bible-study, when Christ-students shall realize that the Author is "mystically present in his Book;" and they shall thus sit at his feet, as Lazarus' sister Mary did, and learn of him. 

The "Broken Bands or Mystery Seals," viz., conditional interpretations-of-scripture, testify to the writer's at-one-ment in self-denial and consecration with the Bible prophets; for a vigilant sentinel guards every treasured mystery; and Christlike overcoming and character accompanies the possession of such coveted knowledge (Jhn.8:32; Rev.5:4-7; 19-10; 21:12; 22:9).

In the construction of this Second Witness, Christ "comes with clouds"-the obscuring mysteries of his Second Advent are indisputably unsealed. The interpretation of the first and second chapters of Genesis indicates the appearance of the Word's feet from amid the (perplexities of mind) clouds of heaven. These, along with the explanation of Job's prophecy, constitute the XV

xvi 

FOREWORD 

"surprise chapters" of this book. Moreover, the "correctinterpretation of the Apocalypse" symbolizes the uncovering of the Word's head. Those portions of the 

Bible remaining unsealed-are represented by parts of the Angel's or Revelation's body "draped in clouds" (Rev.1:7; 10:1).

Why should it be considered an unwarranted statement that, "Christ's multiplication or plurality — the bringing of many sons unto glory"-should constitute his second-coming? Yet a little while and THE WORLD 

SEETH ME NO MORE; but ye see me. I go to my

Father and ye see me (the original-Word made flesh, Gen.24:5-6; Jhn.14:19; 16:10,16; Heb.2:10-11) no more.

Truly, in the fullness of the Godhead or Christ's mindwe become "responsive members" of his body, of his flesh, and of his bones (our bodies are spiritually his, Eph.5:23-20).

The profession of possessing Christlikeness is, at least, as old as Job's day, whose book is reputed to be the first of the Bible library. He knew that his Redeemer then lived, and that he, in Job's "final revelation"—just as the writer now testifies-should (stand upon the earth) be made manifest in Job's overcoming the body. That this should constitute "God in his flesh," whom he should recognize, and not another. It required no greater quantity, nor other quality of faith, for the O.T. faithful to look forward to the cross of Christ's at-one-ment, than is needed for us to look backward (Job 19:23-27; 

38:7; and God, the Father, is no respecter of persons.

He certainly loved them as much and as efficaciously, as we folk of the N.T. dispensation. A spiritual or psychological construction placed upon the Word of prophecy... makes it accessible to all. The literalist—in the narrowness of his partiality, or misrepresentation of divine love becomes a false-prophet, in that "he restricts the promise of Christ's coming-again to a single person" and a select (generation) few, who sometime receive him; disappoints the faith of many, and so discounts the value of providence. God's PLAN reveals (himself) INFINITE wisdom and love... SO judge. Multitudes of saints, all down through the ages, have unquestionably obtained the experience that is here analyzed and commended; but, as the Word forewarned, the day (or revelation) of the Lord came upon the sleeping or unwatchful . . . as a thief in the night, because

FOREWORD 

xvii they disregarded the fact that the kingdom of Godwhere Bible prophecy is spiritually fulfilled, and Christ reigns-was within them, and they mistook his reappearance as literal and general, instead of spiritual, personal, and optional with every son of God, who recognizes in Christ his "exemplary self." Now, by having received "this present-truth," one can the better appreciate his or her individual-privilege; and the number of the ELECT should multiply apace. 

The Word was made flesh; and the Firstfruits of the resurrection were literally revealed; but were said to be divine; and that seemed to be an attribute wholly beyond human attainment. Arius the Libyan proved this to be a delusion, by the Word itself; for which he was excommunicated, exiled, and poisoned by his opponents. 

In the rejection of his "advanced views," Antichrist was enthroned in the Church as Christ's-vicegerent. Now, in this Time-of-the-end "reappearance of Arianism," judgment of the Antichrist, and assured Second Reformation, he is vindicated and reinstated as the "Full-Gospel Messenger."

Prophecy has decreed that these "measuring Witnesses" should prove to be a great blessing to the saints (Rev. 

11:10); no doubt, via revealing the man-of-sin and the subsequent return of their Lord. It is the faultless functioning of all the members of Christ's body or Church" which materializes his "absolute presence" upon earth (1 Cor.12:17-31). The Pope, as usurping head-member, roaming or catholic bishop-to the exclusion of universal equity—becomes the Antichrist. Arianism, in contrast, restores the primitive - Head or "first love" of the Church.

Antichrist must be revealed, prior to Christ's coming to either the individual Christian or the Church, that the shortcomings of a secular-kingdom might be compared with the advantages of a spiritual-kingdom. 

These specimens of "spiritualized prophecy" are indisputable proof that the Bible is inspired. What book of religion-other than the Bible-contains a hiddenmystery of comparative character-building formulas; or a historical-allegory of spiritual-progress, which can be intelligently worked out, in detail, via analyzing personal-experience?

As the Bible was originally-written by many men, under inspiration, the burden of its final-interpretation

xviii 

FOREWORD 

must, also, be borne by numerous Spirit-enlightened persons. But, be assured, reader, should you, for one, discover that you have such a gift-and-resultant-calling, neither perfect-insight nor satisfaction will crown your research, until you have attained your Time-of-the-end; for such is the gist-of-promise (Dan.12:4,9,10). Then you shall see as you are seen; and comparisons of deadletter and fulfilled prophecies will come clear and accurate (1 Cor.2:13; Rev.19:10). However, don't delay, delve into the hidden-mystery of Christ within you, the hope of glory, immediately. The Father is anxious to reveal him unto you. Practice should gradually increase your aptitude for such service, and stimulate progress. 

Bear in mind that no prophecy-of-scripture is of any private-interpretation, therefore, it is ALL yours to investigate and explain. Search the pages of recollection for relative or shadowed facts (Col.2:17; Heb.10:1).

Cowardly Christians are the disobedient ones, having an accusing-conscience which does not readily beget confidence. Fearlessness and faith are born of pure-love, which is manifested in obedience. Patient investigation will find valuable nuggets-of-truth, long overlooked. If, in your whole life-time, you are unable to add but meager facts to those already revealed-the "good cause" is indebted to you at least THAT much. You SO have secured a legacy-of-everlasting-consolation. 

Quit "just reading" the Word, study (Him) it. Don't accept anybody's version off-handed. Feed, for yourself, in the abundant green-pasture prepared by our thoughtful Shepherd-for ALL his sheep. Commentaries are confusing and, quite often, misleading. Let the other prophets explain what any one of them doesn't quite make clear; but stick to the Word... it speaks for itself. 

When fulfilled, the seven Signs-of-the-times are the "master keys" to Bible study. They unlock all doors and gates leading to the vast unexplored treasure-fields of the mystic inner-realm of infinite-knowledge of eternalspirit. Be diligent to acquire these, as soon as ever possible. They represent full heartedness and martyr-devotion to "all that's Christ's."

Never envy another member-of-the-body, function where you belong; if in doubt, "entire consecration" will dispell uncertainty. Covet eagerly the most-useful gifts, not the most-spectacular. Possibly, you can better teach the truth, than those who have had the time and pa-

FOREWORD 

xix tience to dig it out. If so that's your forte! Develop it unto perfection; and most-wisely glorify your Head. "Bind up the testimony, seal the law among my disciples:" viz., bring all the gifts-of-the-body into service; let us prove to the world that Christ is actually and practically present in his members!-Isa.8:16.

The "solitary Christ" of the first advent-failed to meet the popular - conception of a hoped-for great - deliverer. So, too, "his multiplied second-advent" must bring sorrow to the hearts of the literalists. "Popish ostentation" would have given them a secretly cherished worldly advantage-if SO, the Lord had come. But, after a long and careful consideration-as in the first instance there will, of course, be a general reconciliation to the truth; and Wisdom and divine Love will be seen to have won out. A literal-interpretation placed upon the Word-is a "sure sign" of literal-mindedness. 

To the non-spiritual the natural and the material, alone, are realistic and important. The Lord has "well said" that his thoughts are not man's thoughts, nor his ways man's ways.

It is the "spirit of antichrist" to deny-that very God, or Christ, can and should be reproduced in all of us! It is as if Abraham, the father of the Jewish race, should arise and claim to be of greater-importance, than ALL of his seed. Our Saviour and Example sowed a naturalbody; and he reaps a multiplied spiritual-body or Church, and a seasonable harvest-of-sons. We, too, sow our bodies for an influential-resurrection (1 Jhn.3:16). The gift, without the giver, is barren. And no natural-Father is jealous of his son, because he emulates his character and becomes manly, noble, virtuous of heart, and useful in his generation. Antichrist makes God "so holy" that mortals cannot possibly approach him in the final splendor of developed immortality. Such a Christ, to the 

Christian, could mean nothing but a "tantalizing example"! But the Christian-religion is a “family affair,' and the Father's great Christmas-present to this world, was nothing less than HIMSELF. revealed unto us through our elder-brother, Jesus-that we should pattern after him, even unto scriptural perfection. "9 Spiritual - things are only comparable with spiritual and-in the production of these Two Witnesses the writer has, unavoidably, interpreted the Word out of his own experience. XX 

FOREWORD 

or seven steps-of-multiplication are now unsealed in the light of revelation, due to be received during this literal or shadowing Time-of-the-end-an age of electrical inventions - figurative of the "personal experience" of binding Satan, as lightning (Lk.10:18; Rev.20:2); delineating-just what part of the law and prophecy has been fulfilled and-what part is yet future. There is no such thing as a "secret formula," to the saint who has gone on to a state of perfect-love; who has passed the 

Holy and Most Holy "mystery-curtains or clouds-ofinexperience." This experience casts out ALL fear; and dares to lie in Christ's bosom, and declare that man can be made righteous-even as his Pattern was righteousand, too, here in this present world. The "Apostle of this promise" and Arius, SURELY had kindred spirits! 

Our Law or Example said, "Behold, I come quickly!" but let no Christian suppose that that implies easily; he must be willing to make a Christlike-sacrifice: to die daily, viz., at the command of each "new revelation" of

Truth. The Word of God, or the sword of the Spirit, divides between soul and spirit (there are seven such divisions, changes of soul or mind), and joints and marrow (bending the knees, in humiliation, represents crossbearing; and finds no oiled-up response or prompting in the natural-man). Christ-maturity casts out the "ghost of self;" for flesh and blood cannot inherit the kingdomof-God, and the inner (life) spiritual-man is built up at the expense of (supplants, 2 Cor.4:16) the outwardman.

The bulk of the task of interpreting the Word or eliminating the "mystery clouds" concealing the “inner presence" of His second-coming, as yet, remains undone.

This "Little of the Book Opened" is evidence of the greater - responsibility which automatically falls upon others-better prepared to carry on the good-work, than he who has pioneered the way. Miscellaneous chapters, scattered here and there in the Word, have been selected for examples. . . to prove that the "whole of the Book" possesses a hidden-mystery, which will be solved by virtue of the Church obtaining her Seventh Revelation. 

Thou also, example of man, the heirs of thy interpretations are still talking of thee by the walls and in the doors of the buildings, and speak one to another, every one to his confident, saying, Come, I pray you, and hear what is the plan characterized by the (Architect) Law.

xxi 

And they come unto thee as the pupil cometh, and they sit before thee as my pupils, and they hear thy ininstructions, but they will not follow them: for with their mouth they manifest much approval, but their heart preferreth their literalism.

And, observe, thou are unto them as a befitting sentimental song of one that hath a charming voice, and can play well on the emotion: for they hear thy instructions, but they follow them not. 

And when this cometh to pass, (watch, it will come), then shall they know that an authority hath been among them.-Eze.33:30-33; 1 Cor.3:9-17.

7 This FULL (salvation) GOSPEL doctrine-long buried in typology, and so kept intact-is as reliable as though freshly written by the Creator. Degrees or steps of soul-culture and spiritual-progress are set forth as seven transitions out of the ignorance of inexperience or nights of mentaldarkness, into the succeeding light of revelation or day. TEMPLE-PLAN TYPES THE OVERCOMING LIFE 29 

THE OVERCOMING LIFE AS TYPIFIED BY SOLOMON'S TEMPLE 

EFORE building a house, every wise man plans the details of its construction, so as to best meet the needs or serve the interests of its future occupants 

In the "well-ordered modern-home," we invariably find a parlor or reception room, a dining-room, a kitchen, a cupboard, a pantry, bed rooms, clothes closets, bathroom, etc.; with these we are generally familiar. So, too, may we reasonably expect to find that every detail of God's house, which Solomon built for him, has a significance which may be profitably considered (Eze. 

43:10-12; 44:5; Rev.11:1). It is a serious mistake to suppose that "that literal Temple"-though it has long since ceased to exist-does not have some importantbearing upon our own lives, who are now members of the household-of-faith (Gal.6:10). The Temple Plan un doubtedly reveals "God's universal-method of rearing his spiritual-offspring" during the past, present, and future ages (Jhn.1:12; Acts 17:28; Heb.12:9).

The Word informs us that "in our Father's House there are many mansions;" and that it was needful that he should depart, that he might direct the building of those structures. Accordingly, Solomon's Temple is the 

PATTERN of that character-building which is so often being duplicated by the sons of God (Mat.5:48; Heb. 

2:10-13). And as children are accustomed to call their fathers' house home so we find peace and plenty by "dwelling in God" (Lk.15:17-24; Jhn.14:1-10).

THE THREE TEMPLES: "Solomon's Temple" was a material-structure (mental-picture, model, design), in every particular perfectly symbolizing-to the easy comprehension of the human understanding-the various features and functions of the "Christian's body" (physical-temple of the Holy Ghost.- Jhn.2:19-21; 1 Cor. 

3:11-17; 6:19; 2 Cor.6:16), used in the construction of that enduring and all-important "spiritual-temple, described in Ezekiel's vision, which is a revelation of God's character or image;" Christ being the firstfruits or original-Pattern, the foundation of Christian faith and hope. 

All scripture bears three applications: historical, as relating to literal-facts; allegorical, in its symbolic-sense, viz., types and shadows, rendering those "good-things,

promised or to come,” apparent to the mind; and spiritual, as duplicated by faith in the character. 

As truly as a Christian takes his body with him wheresoever he goes, that surely is he here environed by the courts, pavements, etc. of the Father's house, which were typed by those of Solomon's Temple. Therefore, we should carefully study out and bear in mind its mystery, as revealed in this diagram. The daily spiritual-sacrifices of the sanctuary of the kingdom of Godby a kingly Melchisedec-priesthood of the Is-real seed of Abraham-are emulations of Christ's offering (Heb. 7:1-24; 1 Pet.2:5). ...

The "primal-consideration of every house" is ITS FOUNDATION. Accordingly, as a basis of reasoning, we first enquire why Ezekiel visualized the Temple. upon three terraces. Every Christian, as we well know, is beset with various degrees of a variety of temptations, typed throughout the Bible by "waves of the sea" (2 Sam.22:5; Psa.42:7; Isa.17:12-13; 25:4; 51:15; 59: 

19; Jna.2:3). For every man is tempted, when he is drawn away of his own lust and enticed (Jas.1:14); and human-flesh is principally water or moisture. Little remains of a dead-body when dried in the sun. Any storage-battery must be kept filled with water, when in use, or it will soon burn out.

Water always seeks its own level; and the "high-tide or furthermost approach" of the sea-of-temptation is (the measure of a Christian's overcoming) typed by the platform of the house-of-the-Temple, which represents the Christian's-body. If he can but overcome the foes within the confines of these walls-which are the tendencies of the physical senses-he can most assuredly conquor "the less-potent" on the outside (Rom.7:18-24; 

1 Jhn.2:16-17). "Secondary temptation" or that which is next mostintense or liable to tempt the Christian to backslideis (the sex appeal) that caused by his companion, whose nearness to him is represented by the Inner Court. Deliverance, in this case, is only possible by a mutual interest and agreement to mortify the deeds-of-the-body by a sacrifice of bestiality upon the Altar of Burnt Offering (indicated in the diagram by the letter A.-Rom. 

8:13; 12:1-2; 1 Cor.7:29,37; Heb.13:10,13). Adam's fall came not of his own body, but of the woo-man Eve, within his Inner Court; she first partook of the forbid-

TEMPLE-PLAN TYPES THE OVERCOMING LIFE 31 

den-fruit, then offered it to her husband! (1 Tim.2:14)

The two Singers' Chambers, located in this court, type a pair of cooing lovers (Cant.1:15; 2:14; 5:2; Eze.40: 

44); but the Altar is situated between them-as a silent reminder of their need of (restraint, according to 

Christ's Revelation) a daily-sacrifice (Lk.9:23; 1 Cor. 

15:31): that God is ever their "unseen guest," his presence should be respected, and his will obeyed. Think you that any well-regulated household can afford to ignore or do away with this Altar-of-restraint, or refuse to consult the Creator's plan of domestic affairs? (Rev. 14:4,12,13)

There are three gates leading into the Inner Court: these pertain to the body (south gate), the mind (east gate), and spirit (north gate) of the Christian. All of these afford an avenue-of-approach to temptation. The stair, leading up to the house-of-the-Temple, from the Inner Court, reveals that the Christian has to "lower himself" in his own estimation, or come down-in yielding to any temptation outside of himself. 

Surrounding the house-of-the-Temple is the Separate Place; indicative of the fact that the Christian must "stand alone" in making a decision or choice. Every proposition or question presenting itself before him, should immediately be considered in the light of God'saltar or Christ's sacrifice, standing directly without the door of his house or body, and being the "first-object to meet his vision." It is here represented as the “vitalspot of interest or divine-worship"-as occupying the "very center of the Temple-precinct" (Acts 4:12; Phil. 

3:8; 1 Pet.2:21).

On the same level with the Inner Court, is the Upper Pavement and the Priests' Chambers; these complete the family-circle, for here is the sphere of the couple's offspring. Next to the Christian's bosom-companion from a "standpoint of responsibility"—are his children.

The Priests' Chambers (C) type storage-rooms for physical, mental, and spiritual provisions which must be provided for the family; as indicated by the "three tiers of rooms" that appear one above the other (1 Thes. 

5:23). The shelves or gallery of the upper-row indicate that the spirit is to be catered to expressly-as of final, uppermost, or supreme importance (Eze.42:1-19; Jhn. 

6:63; 2 Cor.4:16-18).

The Inner Chambers, next to the Separate Place, type pantries (Eze.42:13; Mat.4:4; Jhn.6:48-58; Jas.1:5).

The Outward Chambers type wardrobes; for the Christian is clothed with the "priestly-apparel of godliness" or goodly garments-of-righteousness (Ex.28; Isa.49:18; 

Eze.44:19; Eph.5:27; Rev.19:8). These pertain to the four living-creatures or the four outward-aspects of the Christian life. The south group treats with the first and second degrees-of-virtue; those north with the first and second degrees-of-grace. Naturally the Christian eats, drinks and wears what he has on hand: and the long shelves to the south in "memory's pantry" are laden with experience or stores of leviathan meat, sweet manna, Wilderness quail, etc.; while "Promised Land overcoming" gives us corn-bread on the shelf, meal in the bin, pans of milk, jars of honey, cheese, butter, and baskets of lucious fruits-of-holiness, etc. (Deu.8:2-16; Psa.74:14).

The "equal or even division" of the Temple's proportions, to the right and left, lends a perfect balance or comparison to all parts of the Christian life (Mal.4:6): "virtue and discipline" are as necessary as "grace and overcoming," in the expression of godliness. The two kitchens (E.E.-Eze.46:19-20) denote that the "two pairs" of living-creatures require a difference diet; the food that the one eats should not be forced upon the other. Here sacrifices were boiled and baked by the priests.

It would be absurd to expect younger Christians to make like-sacrifices with their elders, or to have the same preferences or tastes. A boiled-sacrifice (water added) is indicative of a fiery-trial which is beneficial or adds to the flesh; and a baked sacrifice (water extracted) is an emblem of a fiery-trial which subtracts from the flesh. Divine chastisement, as a rule, tends to the preservation and the development of our bodies; but in order to perfect character and unfold the soul, the flesh must suffer, for mind and spirit-to which the body is naturally indifferent-supersede it (Heb.12:1012).

The building located to the west of the house-of-the- Temple (H), like a hall-of-records or reference-room, is figurative of the accumulated "after-effects of carrying out the divine-plan of family-life" (Eze.41:15; Mat.7:11; 

12:35; 13:52). It is very essential that the "law of

TEMPLE-PLAN TYPES THE OVERCOMING Life 33 

seed-time and harvest or cause and effect" should be taken into account, as one, be he young or old, naturally likes (is entitled) to know the (wisdom of restraint) reason why he should or should not deny himself, and what is the consequence of obedience or disobedience of God's laws (Pro.24:14; Mat.6:22-23; Rom.6:2223; Gal.6:7-8; 1 Tim.4:8; 1 Pet.3:15).

The Lower Terrace, typing the "third-degree of temptation," is comprised of the Outer Court and the Lower Pavement. The Outer Court, like the Inner Court, symbolizes the meeting-place of the husband and wife; but, in this case, it is "the spiritual Bridegroom and Bride" or Christ and his Church (Jol.2:16; Eph.5:23-33; Rev. 

21:2,9,10). This court, which is laid out like the Upper

Pavement, is where the "whole Church or family of 

God" mingles and feasts together.

The Thirty Chambers (G), located upon the Lower Pavement, type the "executive-aptitude of the Christian or his mental-faculties”—which he relies upon in his week-day business transactions (Eze.40:17-18; Zech. 

11:12-13; Mat.26:15). These are brought into action through contact with the world (profane place, Eze. 

42:20; Heb.13:13), that is located beyond this pavement and without the Temple walls (body or Church of Christ).

The four chambers (F), situated in the extremecorners of the Temple, where the Levites boiled sacrifices for the people like the four horns-of-the-Altar (Psa.118:27)-type self-sacrifice in the interest of the public, and a gradual transition from the least to the greatest of the four living-creatures (Mat.20:25-28; 

Heb.13:16; Rev.4:6-8), or from one degree of usefulness to another in the Church or household-of-God (1 Cor. 

12:31; 1 Tim.3:1-7).

There is also a "fourth-degree of temptation" which besets the Christian, viz., from the low-lying world without. But the Christian is in the world and not of it (Jhn.15:19), therefore, he is committing what is scripturally or spiritually termed fornication, when he compromises his convictions with influences "outside the kingdom" (Jas.4:4). The admission of strangers or unbelievers (blighting effects) within the Temple - walls profanes the Holy Sanctuary (Eze.44:7,9; Rev.21:27), and comes under the head of penalized-sacrilege (Mat. 

5:28; 2 Cor.6:14-18; 1 Thes.5:22; 2 Pet.2:20-22).

The Christian cannot avoid contact with his body, his mate, or his children. And he simply must have the fellowship of the saints in the Outer Court. He is also compelled to do business and associate with the outsideworld, as typed by the Lower Pavement. But to unduly expose himself (open the flood-gates.-Isa.59:19) to "low-tide temptation"-which otherwise could not reach him-is (antichrist) tempting God! (Job.1:10; 38:11; 

Psa.27:5; 31:20; 32:6-7; 91:1-3; 104:9; Pro.8:29; Jer. 

2:25; 5:22; Mat.4:7; 16:18; 1 Cor.10:13).

A Christian's "foremost-duty toward the world"-is to "set it a godly-example, by doing business on-thesquare," as denoted by the formation of the Lower Pavement, in the corners of which are the four chambers, typing the (offices) offerings of the four living or spiritual creatures, exemplifying the fourfold Gospel (Eze. 

46:21-24; Rev.7:1; 21:16).

THE MAGNET IN SOLOMON'S (Wisdom's) TEMPLE 

HE "three stories of side-chambers" in the Temple (Solomon's in replica) of Ezekiel's vision (1 Ki. 

6:5-8; Eze.41:5-11), on either side and at the rear of the house, one arm over against the other, represent magnetic current conductors-the positive and negative of human knowledge, ability and disposition; exemplifying, via good works, Christian-progress in the kingdom-of-God.

Each succeeding group of chambers, larger than that just beneath, symbolizes three successive nights (trials) and days (revelations) of character-creation, of gradually increasing interest and importance. The thirty chambers, on each floor, are figurative of the spiritualapplication of the thirty diverse mental-faculties. The larger dimensions of each ascendant tier bespeaks growth of the tenants and congested conditions below, and the consequent need of larger quarters. It also denotes a wider range of thought and keener insight; in fact, an accumulation of additional treasure, beyond the storage-capacity of the lower (floor) rooms (Isa.1:26; 

49:20; Amos 9:6).

The three days referred to, are either those of the sinner, spent in the development of virtue, under our schoolmaster the law-constituting his leviathan (experience, Psa.74:14; Mat.12:39-40; 13:33) lamp; or the three days of a Christian's growth in grace with his Eveready lamp, aglow by the "extra supply of oil" received at sanctification or midnight (full) consecration, viz., the instruction of the Holy Ghost (Mat.24:44; 25:4; Eph.5:18).

In the second instance, the justified Christian-is depressed by a sense-of-shame, for having repeatedly suffered defeat at the hands of the old-man (Rom.7:24), finding it difficult to part company with the world, take up his cross and follow Christ, make daily-sacrifices and thus build character. With consecration he begins to experience a change-of-heart, as a result of his becoming a party to the "new character-covenant of reason and love" (Heb.10:16): to bring all the tithes into the storehouse, that there should be material wherewith to build the house-of-God (Mal.3:10; 2 Cor.5:1). After receiving the sanctifying power-of-the-Holy Ghost (Lk.

MYSTIC-MAGNET IN THE TEMPLE 

37 

24:49; Acts 1:8) instead of drawing back he can scarcely be restrained from service; for his mind is then undivided and spiritualized.

The "thirty chambers, of each tier," are relatively identified with those above or below, whichever the day's experience may chance to be; and the upper chambers are attained by quietly pushing aside the loosetimbers overhead. The Word specifically states that the flooring of these side-chambers was (is) not fastened to the walls of the house. 

The thirty faculties, like so many germinating-seeds sown in the loose earth, undergoing a process-of-transformation-in order to make room for themselvesthey thrust aside the surrounding clods, in expansion and upward-growth, until finally the sprouting-plants triumphantly poke their heads above the surface of the black-soil, into light and liberty. A hard-fought battle, indeed, but final-victory makes the struggle well worth while (Mk.4:26-32).

The manipulation of the faculties may be likened unto a spiral coil or spring, an elastic-band, an absorbingsponge, like the lungs: anything, in fact, which alternately expands and contracts. Thus they act upon the muscles of the physical-anatomy, as they play back and forth in response to the dictation of the will--which controls the entire body. In the execution of any given task, there is alternately a delicate touch, a severe pressure, an utter relaxation, etc., as in the ebb and flow of the tide of concern, emotion, determination, or sorrow-consequent to the soul-travail of a condemned-sinner-leading up to repentance and the subsequent birth of a son of God, typed by the convulsions of the whale or big-fish, in its persistent efforts to digest Jonah; or the raging flames of hell-fire, to inflict punishment upon the sinless Christ the abominable (righteous Substitute.-1 Pet.1:1819) Branch which would not burn; and was, therefore, cast forth without the furnace (tomb), as not deserving the fate which had befallen him. He was esteemed as "misplaced-material" by the denizens of the underworld (Gal.3:23).

As Jonah was vomited out upon the dry-land; and as Christ was resurrected out of the tomb . . . is the "Christian's company" spurned by the world, once he ceases to be a part of it (Jhn.15:19). A reverse effect

is obtained in the mid-week Christian who fights holiness, in the face of the plain-teaching of God's Word; searing his "cringing conscience" until it slinks back and confesses a lie (Isa.28:17; 1 Tim.4:2).

What a man says by his deeds, enlightens his neighbor far more than any utterance of mere words (Jas. 

2:17-22). To preach effectively we must also work (make good) with our hands.

The woman who had the issue-of-blood, purposed to act or spoke certain words in her heart, and then by touching the hem of Christ's-garment, contact with divinity was established, and the vitalizing health-fluid, from the Wellspring-of-life, came surging through her being.

Thank God for a "practical way of wishing;" and the possibility of lending a helping-hand in the answering of our own prayers! Everything which we do represents the power of human-ingenuity, resident at a distance, in the lofty "watch-tower of mansoul," which keeps saying to the hands, "Yes, no; hold on, let go; reach in, work out; up, down; etc." . . . until the task is completed. There is infinitely more "genuine salvation" in what-we-do, than in what-we-profess; though both channels-of-expression are needed in the communication of light. "By their fruits shall ye know them" (Mat.7:20). 

Our Lord also said, "Believe the works: that ye may know" (Jhn.10:38). 

How wonderful are the multiplied hands-of-Christ!

Willing workers, as members of his body, do his bidding—as having no mind of their own (Mat.24:44; Eph. 

5:23,30); consider, too, the immense-good that results to ourselves and to others. But the privilege of offering a finished temple or character-building unto God, with the all-important "head-stone of the corner" in its place (Mat.21:42),-viz., Christian possessing the fullness-ofthe-Godhead, represents the acme of world-achievement, and the most-potential qualification for Christian service. 

Those who covet earnestly the "best gifts," will say, like Job, "Shall we receive good at the hand of God, and not receive evil?" Faith implies resignation, we must permit Wisdom to work at will, in and through us. 

The "building of character" treats of Wisdom, of which Christ is THE REVELATION (Mat.12:42; 1 Cor. 

1:24,30). He insisted upon having thirty pieces-of-silver

MYSTIC-MAGNET IN THE TEMPLE 

39 

(this metal emblemizes understanding. Pro.25:11; 

Zech.11:12), as a full-compensation for the deliverance of his righteous-person (the purchase-price of the unblemished-Lamb of God. Ex.12:5-13; Jhn.1:29,36).

Joseph, the son of Jacob, who was a type of (his) the penitent-sinner's faith, clothed in multi colors or bright hopes,-necessarily dipped in atoning-blood (Gen.37: 

3,31; Heb. 11:6; Jude 3), was sold by his envious brethren for twenty pieces-of-silver. Both of these innocents were about to depart for Egypt (Gen.37:28; Isa.14:9,15; 

Heb.13:12-13). Joseph, who was progressing, had everything to gain by his humiliation; for there he became "second to Pharoah in authority," and governor over all the land. In contrast, Christ-who was propitiatorily retrograding-had to forfeit his righteousness and become sin, who had committed no sin (2 Cor.5:21), in order that he might figuratively pass beneath the law or without the camp. (Rom.3:19).

This clearly reveals a deficiency of ten talents or faculties to be, somehow, accounted for in Joseph: thirty divided by three, and twenty into two parts-physical, mental, and spiritual considerations-reveals a deficit of one third part in Joseph.

Being an out-of-doors youth, his body was unquestionably sound; and both previous and subsequent events proved that his morals were above reproach. Therefore, we naturally conclude that, in the nature of things, judging more particularly by his "indiscreet boasting," he was deficient in WISDOM (Gen.37:8-11; 1 Thes.5: 

23), which could only be obtained. . . by going on to maturity (Pro.4:18).

Accordingly, character-building or Christian-progress yields understanding (Psa.84:7; Jhn.8:31-32), which insures a proper-balance of body, mind, and spirit; as affecting sense, reason, and conscience; by virtue of an 

Egyptian, Wilderness, and Canaan experience, allegorically speaking.

The two doors, giving access to the three side-chambers of the Temple, from the platform or place-left-over, bespeak a representation of two lamps (Rom.8:1-9; Gal. 

3:23-27). The south-entrance is the door by which the sinner enters (takes up) his leviathan (Job 3:8); or the 

Christian takes up his Eveready. The north-entrance is the door by which Christ passed under-the-law, when he laid down his Eveready or demonstrable-perfection

(Mat.12:39-40; Rev.19:7), and took up his leviathan .. and became the sinner's substitute. It also types the way by which the Holy Ghost enters to bear witness with our spirits, to a full-surrender of the "first-born soul that sinneth;" or to a finished-consecration of the "uld-man's body-of-death" in self-denying crucifixion (Lev.20:7-8; Rom.12:1-2; Heb. 10:14-15).

Backsliders who leave the Holy Land or the Wilderness of imputation, and go down to Egypt for relief (comfort in trial), contrary to divine will and law (Num.14:4; Isa.30:1-3), must, of course, enter by the north-door of their leviathan; and by doing their firstworks over again they, also, find this same door an 

Eveready exit. But, according to Heb.6:4-6; 2 Pet.2:1922, should they return entirely to the world (cast away their confidence or leviathan-lamp of salvation.-Isa. 

9:2; Rev.8:10), it will be impossible to renew their experience and faith; they cannot enter the south-door of leviathan a second time! (Mat.12:31) ...

The Temple Platform, extending forward and eastward, types the feet of the Christian pilgrim; and denotes the direction which one is expected to journey. always onward, toward the Light-of-the-world (Christ. -Jhn.8:12); which, too, infers a steady burning of one's lamp (Isa.62:1; Mat.5:14-16); for that is the condition guaranteeing the "abiding-presence of the Comforter" or oil in the lamp (Mat.25:4).

IT *For none of us (does good) liveth to himself, and no man (does evil) dieth to himself. 

WE ARE GATES TO ONE ANOTHER 

Gen.22:17; Judg.5:8; Psa. 24:7-9; 118:19-20; 

Pro.8:34; 14:19; Jhn.10:7; *Rom. 14:7

T is the folk whom we admit into our lives, which constitute the Temple Gates. Every individual has his or her own personality. Influence is contageous. 

Character is formed unconsciously.

There are three gates to the Temple; and effects of contact are triple: body, mind, and spirit receive permanent-impressions. This denotes the importance of care in selecting our environment, friends, reading, etc.: benefits or injuries . . . unavoidably result, in every instance.

Guests permitted within the confines of the Templewalls, betray the caliber of the Christian's spirituality and perception (Eze.44:7-9). Thanks be unto God for perogatives which may be asserted: that while a man's body is in (his physical life is of) the world, his citizenship and treasure may be in heaven! And although thrown in company, or transacting business with; seeking the salvation of unclean, contaminating folk; compelled by circumstances, which he is powerless to altar, to hear vile conversation, and to witness the unbecoming conduct of the wicked, which is naturally blighting in its effect (Rev.9:2), the Christian remains unsullied -for the reason that, while his mind is busy upon the Lower Pavement, his spirit has taken refuge within the Outer Court (Christian bears in mind that he is a member of Christ's body or Church.-2 Cor.6:14-18); and so remains undefiled (does not fellowship the ungodly, or eat the "unclean flesh" offered him.-Lev.11; 1 Cor. 

10:20-24; 2 Pet.2:12), though the slimy waves-of-temptation dash violently against the intervening walls-ofsalvation (Pro.25:28; Isa.25:4; 51:15; 57:20; 59:19; 

60:18; Jer.31:35; Eze.26:3; 38:11).

It is only by "special permission" (an act of the will) that an impression can pass through "any one of the Three Temple-Gates," to either influence Christian, or to be passed along to his loved-ones within the sacredprecincts of the Temple. These impassable-bounds, fixed by the decree of the Creator, in his infinite wisdom and mercy, are a "certain protection" to all his dear children (Zech.2:8; Lk.16:26).

TEMPLE GATES REVELATION 

43 Again, the Lord warns the Christian to be on his guard, for his foes are often those of his own household (Mat.10:36). Accordingly, when the Church persecutes him (Jhn.16:2; 2 Tim.3:12), he may retire to the innercircle of his family for consolation and backing. But when his children, too, assail his stand for righteousness, he usually finds "safe refuge" with his wife, within the Inner Court. But when his bosom-companion, also, fails him in the ordeal, which should not always be considered impossible, he may now, as a last-resort, take refuge upon the Altar-with none to befriend him but the Lord God alone; who will never leave nor forsake him. (Note: The top-of-the-Altar stands even high er than the Temple Platform.-Psa.61:2-4) 

The Temple has no courts, pavements, nor barriers,unless there is contact between persons and interchange of ideas. Everyone whom we meet, throws out a "challenge of comparative-standards," good, bad, or indifferent, by his sensed-presence. It is a case of give and take, whatsoever is in stock, agreeable or disagreeable. 

The Outer Temple Gates represent chambers-of-imagery (Eze.8:12; 2 Cor.10:5): the "other-fellow's judgment-hall or weighing-station," through which you must certainly pass for inspection—if you in any wise come in contact with him: by sight, hearing or personal acquaintance. Here is where he forms his opinion of you. 

The Inner Gates type "your judgment-hall" where you, as host, should try your best to find the "good-traits of your visitor," not the evil (Mat.7:12).

Some Christians are, of course, more accurate in their computing; some more exacting; and some quite charitable. Your general appearance, your words, your actions, etc., all in their turn, must cross the threshold (E), and file in through the entry (B), and out the door (A). Which gate, all depends upon the direction from which he comes, the nature of his errand, and the amount of confidence which you place in him; one thing sure, he cannot approach you from the gateless west .. except as a memory. ...

The six chambers (C), with windows, bespeak occupancy. Indeed, here is where body, mind, and spiritknowledge of evil, on the one hand; and knowledge of good, opposite of your guest, stand, while they size you up from scalp to toes.

The palm-trees carved upon the side-walls (D), symbolize Christian (growth) characters, including his own. 

These he uses for reference, in comparison with what seems to be the outgrowth of yours (Psa.92:12). In this manner he forms his estimate of what you are; the value of your opinion, advice, etc. (Isa.65:22; Mat.7:1620). Every question, up for consideration, is treated in this same fashion; and each decision rendered, is accounted for by this identical-process. How awful to sear the conscience (cheat the balances.-Mic.6:11; 1 Tim. 

4:2) by hypnotic-introspection: giving the flesh, mind, or even the spirit, excessive or deficient consideration; thus biasing the judgment and admitting the unholy (a lie or enemy.-Deu.33:29; Isa.5:20; 28:17) inside the walls! Immediately, a man has formed his conclusions, they pass out into the porch, where they await the opportunity to express themselves.

Judging from the fact that the Inner Court Gates face outward, and open into the Outer Court (Eze.40:34), while the Outer Court Gates front inwards (Eze.40:9), and also open into the Outer Court-it is thereby apparent that here is the place appointed for the commigling of the saints: this is where the Church of God is located. The Christian lays aside his week-day labor or business, conducted upon the Lower Pavement, where he meets all sorts of people on an equality, also the perplexities and responsibilities of family-life; then he, his companion and children, leave the home (Inner Court and Upper Pavement), for attendance at worship, with others (related by the blood-of-Christ) of God's heirs, who have done likewise; and they all meet upon common-grounds (Jude 3).

The overcoming Christian doesn't seek to the outerworld for his diversion; but with "others of similar inclination," who have also experienced salvation, therefore are alike in choice, they assemble with one accordeach entering, as it were, through the gate and into the Outer Court of the other for divine worship. As the host's and the guest's gates face each other, we realize that Christians cannot consistently judge each other coming, but going: not until they have met together in the Holy Sanctuary, and each has eaten the "bread of the other's testimony."

The significance of the eight steps of the Inner Court Gates, instead of seven, like the Outer Gates, bespeaks

TEMPLE GATES REVELATION 

45 privacy (Eze.40:22,31). The home, as an institution, is one step above the Church-in "point of sacredness;" and no man's domestic-affairs are, in any sense, public considerations. The seven steps of the Outer Gates symbolize the Sabbath-day perfection which Christ looks for in every member of his Bride or Church. 

The reason why the Temple Gates are closed and "bridge the Two Pavements" (Eze.40:18), is that neither a Christian's children, nor his secular calling--are in any wise to be permitted to interfere with the king's (overcomer's) business of character - building, which stands preeminently above money matters (Mat.16:26), and the consideration of his offspring (Isa.56:4-5).

Christ and the Church before business, always; husband (A-Lord) and wife, before the children!

Christian is commanded to keep the East Gate of the 

Temple shut (Eze.44:2-3); for the reason that the Lord (Law) his God has entered in by it: viz., Christian is not to take any one for an example, except Christ (Jhn. 

21:22; 1 Pet.2:21); and not to allow anyone to argue him out of his "hope in Christ;" that the Lord's Word settles the controversy: it is always to be final. Therefore, this leaves only "two gates remaining:" the south (negative flesh), and the north (positive spirit), for the Christian to guard. When any question of propriety arises, Christian (the prince.-Gen.32:28) has but to weigh it in the "light of the life-of-Christ," the infallible-Word of Wisdom (Pro.8:22-34; Jhn.1:1-4), or eat bread before the Lord (Jhn.6:31-35).

Therefore, everything is either evil or good (Deu.30: 

15,19; 1 Pet.3:12), as decreed by the Lord, in his Word; and it is either to be rejected or accepted, because, "Thus saith the Lord" (2 Ki.1:4), or, "It is written."-- Mat.4:4,7,10.

ALTAR REVELATION

THE ALTAR'S SYMBOLIC MEASUREMENTS 

HE border from 1 to 2 is a cubit, corresponding to TH the height of the lower body-bosom, and each of the (daily spiritual sacrifices) seven steps. Therefore the ascending - subtraction from all of the four faces (Eze.1:6), or width dimensions, types a decrease of physical control (Rom.8:13; Gal.5:24; Eph.3:6), until twelve cubits remain, which signifies the body's (promised-land's, Rom.8:23; Phil.3:21) ability to seasonably yield a full-crop of (12 virtues or graces, Jhn.15:8; 

Rom.6:22; Rev.22:2) fruit, the harvest of a 12 x 12 or 

144 thou-sand.-Rev.7:4; 13:1.

A up, down, all-around shrinkage of (a cubit types a humiliating or prostrating consecration) one day's sacrifice, from the length and breadth dimensions of each foundation, totals six work-days of physical toil: a (week's self-denial) full reed or growth.-Eze.41:8; Rev.15:1.

The gutter-border, of the body-bosom and the spiritsettle, types troughs which receive the overflow of blessing descending upon the offering. Whereas the lower mind-settle, having no retainer, indicates that "it alone is wrought upon" (ALTERED), by virtue of the sacrifice being consumed or transformed by the fiery Holy 

Ghost (Mat.3:11; Acts 2:3-4; Rom.12:1-2; Eph.4:23), balancing in Wisdom sense of human reasoning vs. superior-intuition. inferior The construction of the Altar, therefore, plainly symbolizes the fourfold-Gospel, and the threefold-treatment of sacrifices offered thereon, for the perfection of "that mind which was also in Christ Jesus."-Phil.2:5-6; Col. 2:9-10. 

Notice their respective alinement by the Law, and the comparative value of body, mind and spirit—as typed by the size of the Altar's three foundations: Mind is allotted twice the prominence of body; and spirit is accredited four times (4 in 1, Eze.1:5) the importance of flesh, besides ascendency over both mind and body. 

In his "upward walk with God" Christian soon learns "what comprises the mountain or pyramid of complete - consecration."-Gen.5:24; Mat.23:19; 1 Pet. 2:21.

Their Respective Anointings Cause the Wise and Foolish Virgins to Burn as Lamps.

8. And by Christlike deeds (Jas.2:18,22,24) they progress toward their four goals; and they four have their objectives and their (experiences) testimonies. 

9. Their testimonies corroborate (do not conflict with) one another; they act when they make up their minds; they do not heed (counter attractions, Isa. 50:7; 

Gal.1:16) opposition. 

10. As regards the similarity of their objectives, they four have the objective of a perfect-Man (Exemplary bishop, 1 Cor.4:9; 2 Cor.3:18; 1 Jhn.3:1-3), and the objective of a (lion, Job 10:16; Isa.31:4) evangelist hunting converts, on the right (*spiritual, positive, Psa.11:30; Jas.5:20) side: and they four have the objective of a (ox, 1 Cor.9:9-11; 1 Tim.5:18) pastor on the left (dual, negative, 1 Cor.12:31; Heb.5:11-14) side; they four also have the objective of a (eagle, Psa.103:5; 

Isa.40:31; Acts 20:34-35) provident-Christian. 

11. Thus are their objectives and their testimonies spiritual (Jhn.4:23-24; 6:63); two transitions of every creation are of one mind, and two (the first pair, Ver. 

23; 1 Cor.13:9-13; 15:42-54) favor their bodies. 

12. And they tend everyone to his own business: where the Holy Spirit is to work, they open the way (Rom.10:13-21); and they do not (shrink, falter, Lk. 

9:62; Heb. 10:38) draw back. 

13. As for the employment of the spiritual creations, their business is to scatter conviction (fire-brands, Jol. 

2:3; Heb.1:7), their service reveals the wise virgin's (Eveready lamp) and the foolish virgin's (leviathan lamp, Mat.25:1-13; Rev.11:4) minds: zealousness fluctuates among Christians; and their love is evident, and out of the love goes forth influence (Acts 2:37-43; 1 Cor.11:31). 

14. And the spiritual creations attract and captivate (encircle, Isa.55:11) as quick as lightning. 

15. Now (at the Time-of-the-end, Dan.12:4,9; Jhn. 

11:56), as I observe the spiritual creations, alternately one witnesses to the Church of spiritual creations, about his fourfold objectives. 

16. And the object of the discourses and their effect is the development of contentment (edification, encouragement: the beryl is a bluish-green gem, restful to the eyes, a six-sided prism, typing the desired sixth-degree perfection, 1 Cor.14:25-40; Rev. 3:7-13); and they four * Hebrew reads the reverse of English: from right to left.

have one business: and their influence and their service contribute toward supplanting (a wheel types enlightenment via progress) a spiritual (going) life in place of a carnal life. 

17. When they testify, they testify about their foursquare interests: they shame not when they testify (Rom.1:16; Rev.21:16). 

18. As for their messages (flashes of perception), they are so comprehensive (inspired, Mat.10:20; 1 Pet. 

4:11) that they are indisputable; and their flashes (clear the darkness, Phil.2:15) reveal many facts relating to them four (food for all). 

19. And when (where) the spiritual creations travel, the (flashes) witnessing is continued by them; and when (where) the spiritual creations are put out of the Church, the lamps (testimonies) are put out.

THE CELIBACY OF A CONSECRATED PRIESTHOOD 

´EITHER let the eunuch say, Behold, I am a dry tree. For thus saith the Lord, Unto the eunuchs that keep my sabbaths, and choose the things that please me, and take hold of my covenant: even unto them will 

I give in mine house and within my walls, a place and a name better than sons and daughters: I will give them an everlasting name, that shall not be cut off.- Isa.56:3-5.

For there are some eunuchs, which were so from their mother's womb; and there are some eunuchs which were made eunuchs of men; and There be Eunuchs, which have made themselves Eunuchs for the Kingdom of Heaven's Sake. He that is able to receive it, let him (by all means) receive it.-Mat.19:12; 1 Cor.7:37.

These are they which were not defiled with women; for they are virgins. These are they which follow the 

Lamb wither soever he goeth. These were redeemed from among men, being the firstfruits unto God and the Lamb. And in their mouth was found no guile; for they are without fault before the throne of God.-Rev. 14:4-5.

These scriptures prove that celibacy is a commendable Nazarite "vow of chastity," and tends to "soul-development or godliness" which is profitable unto all things, having promise of the life that now is and that which is to come.-1 Tim.4:8.

ND the Lord caused a deep sleep to fall upon A-dam, and he slept: and he took one of his (principles) ribs, and closed up the flesh instead thereof. 

And the rib, which the Lord God had taken from man, made he a woman, and he brought her unto the man. 

And A-dam said, this is now bone of my bones, and flesh of my flesh: she shall be called woo-man, because she (represents or) was taken out of man.-Gen.2:21-23.

Man's conscious and subconscious minds involve a dua (e) l-existence. The sex of the first is masculine; that of the second is feminine, and is the "creative-maternal mind of motherhood," governing respiration (breathing of the lungs) and circulation (pumping of the heart). 

Not only utter-relaxation or sleep, gives the "woo-man self" the ascendency, but the yielding to any natural desire.

CELIBACY REVELATION 

69 A cask is as A-dam, forming an inner reservoir of staves and hoops. The removal of any one of these ribs (reclining a stave, standing a hoop) would naturally cause a leakage. The predominate instinct of a wooman, made from A-dam's "rib or self-sacrifice"-is love; and, God is (spiritualized, Heb.12:9; 1 Jhn.4:8,16) love.

A "male child," originally, has as much natural-affection as a female; but the character of his environment and assumed responsibility, calls for "its replacement" by a fleshly-worldly spirit of self-defense or reliance, conquest, and acquisition-diametrically opposing a culture, or even tolerance, of the divine nature . . . explaining his general-indifference to spirituality (Gen.3:18; 

Isa.45:7). Such constitutes the commended and soughtfor manhood of the human race. This robs A-dam of his rib, first-love or "otherwise natural-concern" for innerdivinity or childbearing, so conspicuously exemplified by the uncensured woo-man (1 Tim.2:11-15; Rev.2:4; 12:2).

But in the process of his religious devotion, as part of Christ's Bride "this instinct” is restored unto him in the fourfold sense of a mature or perfected divinelove (Eph.5:22-23; Col.3:14; 2 Pet.1:3-4; 1 Jhn.4:18), which counteracts his misfortune of a confused-judgment, and aversion for spiritual things (Pro.23:7; Isa. 

1:26; Rom.6:16; 1 Cor.2:14). Martyrs have proven that no man is so manly as "he who dares to be Christlike." 

On the otherhand, an affeminate manhood of worldlycompromise is what prevents the sinner from claiming his "birth-right."-Pro.22:13; Jhn.3:3.

Is it any wonder, that the apostle Paul would not suffer a woo-man to "usurp authority over a man"? He, of course, referred to himself, as an example to other men, i.e., he would not allow his weaker sex or self (slavish desires of the subconscious, deep-sleep or ribrobbing mind) to overcome him.-1 Cor.9:27. 

Yes! that which makes the female-of-mankind so fascinating, is her "unconcealed good-or-godly love;" and a foreign masculine - masquerading of apparel, bobbed hair, smoking, tippling, etc., only tends to lower her in the estimation of the opposite sex. Holy chastity and modesty should be the most prized of ALL her assets. 

He that is unmarried careth for the things that belong to the Lord, how he may please the Lord. But he that

is married careth for the things that are of the World, how he may please his wife.-1 Cor.7:32-33.

Yet outward-celibacy is no more essential to Christlikeness, than an outward-circumcision of the flesh; both of them, in the true, are of the heart (Rom.2:28-29).

But the formal-restraint of the Roman-Catholic clergy, reveals the perfect-type of "from laity to pope or Godhead"-seeking a "visible-Christ or lost first-love." 

Pauline-doctrine condemns "sensual pleasure-seeking or worship of the beast and his image,"as incompatible to spirituality. A woman "usurping authority over a man" implies negative-manliness or effeminacy; but sex is not meant to be a bar to "widowed old-man dead" spiritual-leadership; universal results-thus far-fully justify this statement (Rom.6:6-7; Gal.3:28; 1 Tim.5: 

3,5,9,10). As regards "practical-celibacy" the Apostle suggests that "the married live as though they were not married" (1 Cor.7:29). Job was a man of family, nevertheless God said that he was perfect (Job 1:1-2; 42: 

13-16). Certainly, no A-dam shall be restrained from overcoming his misconception or, winning back his lostrib, i.e., his "virgin-love principle" for a Christ-child or predestinated spiritual-manhood (Isa.7:14-16; 13:7-8; 

21:3; Rev.12:1-2); and, in consecrated soul-travail he, too, may develop many sons "through the gospel."- 

Isa.66:8; Rom.8:14-23; 1 Cor.4:15; Gal.4:19.

Thus, we see that "celibacy treats of a dual-manhood:" the negative or false, via compromise, is a confused antichrist; and the positive or scripturallyinferred, via overcoming for the seed-of-promise or oldage revulsion, is predestinated to a perfect or exemplary 

Revelation of Christ (Rom.8:29-20; 9:7-9; Rev.2:26-28; 12:5).

The wholly-spiritualized individual is a true celibate, of the neuter-gender, or like the working bee, "mentallysexless." To such a Christian, the preferences of God's judgments are sweeter than honey and the honeycomb; and the gathering of them is pastime or heaven.—Psa. 

19:10; Gal.3:28.

The "camouflaged politics" of the Roman Catholic hierarchy are similar to those of any other world power. 

The Antichrist man-of-sin, as Christ's vicegerent or the visible-head of the Christian Church, is no more a spiritual-ruler than the "now vasal" king of Italy. The Vatican City is integrant (police protection proves it) to the literal Roman municipality. The Pope's throne is material, his court also, and its attendants. So are his allover-the-globe bishopric, cardinal constituency, and his "church-member citizens." In fact, the entire administration of his world-wide authority is exercised quite like that of the ancient Cæsars-of which his, is indisputably the more affluent and extensive. 

The subjects of this universal empire-though professing loyalty to their respective countries — render homage, first of all, unto Popedom and its human sovereign. And the Catholic clergy are the "faithful publicans or tax-gatherers of material-revenue," which is exported for the maintenance of their favorite prince. 

Such is the nature of the "kingdom divided-againstitself" which the literalist Protestants claim that Christ is soon to establish upon earth; the impossibility of which is providentially made manifest in Popedom (Mat.12:25). No man can serve (conflicting governments, viz., of like kind) two masters. He will hate the one, and love the other; or else he will hold to the one, and despise the other. Ye cannot serve (spirit) God and (man's image) mammon.-Mat.6:24; 22:19-20; Rev. 13: 14-15.

Two TESTAMENTS CONTRASTED 

109 

OLD TESTAMENT vs. NEW TESTAMENT 

ITH the purpose in mind of avoiding a "thought- WT lessly abrupt-exit" out of the one, and entrance into the other of the testaments, we are here making a few comparisons of the two covenants. It's the crossof-Christ which divides between the dispensations of law and grace (Job 38:4-7). It was his shed blood that terminated those former typifying offerings of bulls and goats. One half of the world's history points-forward to the Saviour's birth; and the other half, or balancebackward. 

Nor is there any "B.C." or "A.D.," for us, until Christ is reborn in our hearts. The Aaronic "priesthood of types" treats of literal sacrifices; and the succeeding 

Melchisedec "priesthood typified" treats of inner spiritual-sacrifices. 

The visible types and shadows of the O.T. are truly essential to an understanding of the invisible good-orspiritual "things to come" of the N.T. The Jew, of the former, fell short of the glory of God-whenever he failed to spiritualize his worship. And the Christian, of the latter, is blind to his spiritual advancement when he fails to consider the "eye-opening or relative type." The fulfilled law of reason and love is thus comparatively witnessed, as a source of incentive and assurance.

The prefiguring antediluvian mist that went up from the earth . . . to water the whole face of the ground (Gen.2:6), represented a "confirmation of the believer's faith or his discernment of a hidden Mystery," back of his physical-sacrifice. Faith is never exercised-except for a "visualized or hoped-for substance and object."- 

Job.22:28; Rom.4:16-25; Heb.11:1.

Every disciple of Christ must take up and bear his Mystic, not literal, cross; and must Mystically, not literally, eat his Master's flesh and drink his blood. The emblems of the sacrament are the Mystic elements or reminders of Christ's broken-body and his shed-blood, which propitiate or meet the requirements of the law, viz., an at- one- ment of reconciliation in agreement; wholly impossible in unbelief. The second - coming of

Christ is a Mystic coming, in his inner-kingdom, via a mental... nevertheless "as truly painful" duplication of his sacrifice. And outwardly fruitful of godly thoughts, words, and deeds.-Heb.13:16; Jas.1:27.

He who prophesies "coming spiritual-events in Christ's kingdom❞—is as truly a prophet as any of the major or minor prophets of Old Testament times, who foretold the conquest or defeat of armies, the rise and fall of kingdoms, also the doom of cities and nations.

A punishment or calamity, "visited upon the individual," is just as dreadful and disastrous a judgment, insofar as "his (the ego's) interests" are concerned, as though a populous city, nation, or the whole-world were involved. And his good-fortune, in avoiding such, should be "greatly-appreciated news" to him.

When we Christians exhort and warn sinners to flee the wrath to come; penalize rebellion against the divine will and law, and the neglect of so-great salvation whether upon the good and all-sufficient authority of God's Word, or in the unction of the Holy Ghost-we are prophesying with a "single or double portion" of that same-spirit which rested upon Elijah. 

The body, without the spirit, is dead; and a type, or dead-letter constitutes the "visible-body of the spiritual or Mystical Word" (Jhn.6:63; Jas.2:26). The repealed law of ordinances-though no longer binding-retains, as much as ever, an "educational value." But its observance will leave an aching-void when substituted, Antichrist fashion, for the prescribed transforming N.T. "worship in the spirit." Moreover, God is not the God of the dead, but of the living. Therefore, whatever application the scripture has to you and me that's its "correct or spiritual sense.' 99 Another "vital point" regarding the prophecies of 

Christ's second-coming: we should not confuse O.T.

Jews of the literal Jerusalem, with N. T. Shews or witnesses of the spiritual Jerusalem (Gal.4:25-26), for the former are not all of spiritual-Israel, or of the promised-seed of faithful Abraham (Rom.2:28-29; 9:6-8). It is absurd to suppose that the Christian's God should claim for his own "those who despise and reject him." 

God's chosen people under-the-law were chosen types; those under-grace are chosen antitypes.

SUPPLEMENTARY REVELATIONS OF THE ANTICHRIST HE interpretation of Jude 4 reads: 

For there are ascertainable men-of-sin (succeeding popes, Jer.5:26-31; Jhn.10:1) climbing into the sheepfold like thieves and robbers, who were before their usurpation prophetic Old Testament types, antichrist men-of-sin, (like Judas) exploiting the authority of our Example selfishly, and (like Peter) denying the supreme 

Law... Godliness, and our Law . . . Sinless Christianity.

The Three Undermining Foundation Denials of Antichrist's still unrepentant Peter-soul-mate, or unprotested heads (personified devils, Pro.23:7; 1 Jhn.3:15) of Protestantism are: (1) The Protestant Church was losing or denying its "first love," or doctrine of the "Word made flesh," which is the essence of Arius' teachings, by not bringing “his bones" along with her in the First Reformation or, Resurrection of Christ's-body out of Antichrist apostasy (Gen.50:25-26). (2) Secondly: by not rejecting Antichrist's Nighcoalition-proselytizing doctrine, in its Reformation, the Church was, in self-defense, denying or substituting, with an oath-of-fidelity, to offset numerical opposition, the need of (soul-travail or a sorrow-for-sin) a spiritual-birth in order to become a Christian, and was thus placing itself on a par with Catholicism. This indiscriminate practice of letting down the bars of the sheepfold, is also the "intermediate or compromising denial." (3) The third and final denial perjures and profanes the believer, as revealed in "Christian Science" so-called, which denies the (need of judgment or repentance, 2 Cor.7:8-11) reality of sin; so making in mind an image of false-security, and defeating the "true-science, or all three foundation - principles" of Christian character building: a knowledge, sorrow, and repentance of sin, or the fear (stop) and hope inspiring effect of Christ's law and (promises) prophets, who became sin that he might reveal in us its presence, nature; then optional twofold destruction. 

Every backslider in so denying his Lord-lowers himself, one rung at a time, until he lands at the bottom of the ladder, in hell . . . without the camp or heavenly communion, where Christ, in his stead, made an at-one-ment for his sins.

Christ's elect-Bride or sanctified body-of-members, in order to obtain the mind of the spiritual-Head, will, with the crucified body-of-sin, also steadfastly reckon its representative, the man-of-sin, to be dead--by ceasing to think independently, or to take the law in their own hands; being content to follow His Revelation, as he did the Father's. The roaring-lion or bluffing-champion of dead-carnality will then cringe before its master-spirit. 

This conquest (Second Coming or installment of Protes tantism, Rev.14:8; 18:2) also unseals the remnant of

Christ's Revelation, and the prophecies dependent upon it for fulfillment.

The man Judas schemed to provide for Christ's literalbody, by capitalizing it-as the (roaming, Isa.19:11) 

Roman bishop now functions in our Lord's spiritualbody or Church.

The man Peter thought to defend Christ's body, and himself one of its members; both marked for sacrifice. These typifying men-of-sin were acting upon their own initiative, virtually ignoring the Master as dead, and unable to further dictate a Church policy or further conduct his own household affairs; considering, but not consulting his will, for fear of its conflicting with their own. So usurping his authority and making in mind a "blasphemous or lying image" of the first beast or original sacrifice-for-sin.

The Protestant Church has truly reformed, insofar as (the breaking of her papal neck or alliance goes, Eze. 

28:4-5,16) condemning the Judas love of money, root of all evil or principle man-of-sin propensity, of "making merchandise out of Christianity" could lift her. This constitutes the Bride's First Resurrection out of Antichrist apostasy. But where are any indications of a reforming Peter's broken and contrite heart, or repentance for having thrice denied his progressive cross-bound Lord?

The Master informed this egotistic disciple that, the cock would crow twice, after he had denied him thrice; and at this Time-of-the-end, the principle of the three denials is more than ever exercised in the Church. A NATURALLY STINKING BRIEF 

T first, the thought of fellowshiping a reclaimedmortal is repulsive; and we, at best, are disposed to place him upon probation-but as he proves himself worthy, he rises in our estimation, and wins our admiration and commendation. 

To know just what the "dead-body of Arianism" smells like, one has but to poke his nose between the covers of our modern encyclopedias. The way this friendly-doctrine: "our brother-in-the-faith, our mainstay, our bread-winner," has been misrepresented, is certainly appalling!

One should hesitate long before believing the lies current about a living-person and, turn a deaf ear to those circulated about a defenseless dead-man. At least, we might give him the benefit of the doubt. Yet the truth is neither altogether in what a man's friends, nor his enemies say. And, in a matter of vital-doctrine, we should most certainly study both sides of the question,then squarely and fairly decide the issue for ourselves, in the light of reason.

How do we recognize those aforementioned deliberate or misconceived lies? Via a comparison with "what appears here." If an authentic-record of Arius and his beliefs had of been handed down to us... they would have, unavoidably, coincided with "what is now revealed;" how, possibly, could they have been otherwise? He lived a faultless life; had the same scriptures to study, and the same Holy Ghost to teach him. If the opposition has been compelled to concede so many good points in a so-called heretic and his condemned creed, and the Church-pendulum has swung so often and so persistently in his favor-there is adequate cause to believe that "the truth was not all admitted." 

Refuse such profane and old wives' fables or conjectures as, a "self-created and always-existing Father, and a coordinate-sonship." Both the dead-letter and the spirit of the Word well justify the conclusion that-as God's first-begotten son, our Saviour and Pattern is-it was the original-intention of the Father-of-spirits to create all of his offspring, in the same image and in like manner.

Today the world has Arianism "fresh from the hand of God." Take it or reject it-for what it actually repre-

A NATURALLY STINKING BRIEF 

141 sents. The past affords but a smattering of this muchmooted doctrine. What is now submitted is destined to settle, for all time-who is right, and what was wrong. 

Please, for comfort's sake, let us all-as much as at present possible, and soon, altogether-break away from that disagreeable-stench which lingers about Lazy Arius' tomb. It is the soul, within that resurrected-body of his, that most concerns us. The same spirit of Jesus-in-his- 

Church, that demanded this miracle,-draws us apart from every environment of unpleasant-memories. As one or the other of his sisters, let us try to forget those musty grave-clothes; and rapturously embrace the "now sanctified" doctrine (brot-her) which formerly introduced Christlikeness to the world. 

The indisputable-cause of Arianism's temporary-setback-is revealed in the mystic-interpretation of the ensuing chapter of John's gospel.

Christ, or a mature-fulness of the Godhead." Its apparent symbolism and evident need-of-interpretation, has impelled thousands of Christ students to attempt the solution of its mysterious contents. Naturally, each commentator thought that His analogy was correct, or he shouldn't have publicly expressed himself; the writer, too, being no exception to the rule. 

The reader will, necessarily, notice occasional similarities between, what appears here and elsewhere; but this eventual "spiritual rendering" will quickly be recognized as wholly foreign to the past popular "historical treatment" of the vision. This is primarily due to the writer's attempt to "stick to his text," or relate dead letters of typology with the corresponding inner-spiritual events, places, and things; and thereby justify or find himself. 

The prophecies of the Book of Job, pertaining to our Time-of-the-end inventions, have had a "literal fulfillment;" but the plain "psychological facts" of the Apocalypse must ever remain a mystery to the uninitiated Christian.

Shame on him, who stubbornly refuses to acknowledge the importance, or even the possible-existence of "that which, alone, can have any lasting value or bearing upon his eternal destiny;" who knows the world without... to the exclusion of the kingdom-of-heaven within, and among the brethren or associate sons of God. 

When the elect Bride-of-Christ sees herself imaged or mirrored, in this Revelation of her Lord's second coming -as the interpreter here reflects-her joy and mutual concern will know no bounds.-Rom.11:7-10; Rev.11:10.

In order to adequately appreciate This Revelation of The Apostle, Who Tarried Until Christ Came, as hereinafter witnessed (Jhn.21:22; Acts 4:20), one should seek his more intimate acquaintance via his gospel and epistles, which are also prophetic. As he received the "fulfilled-promise of the Master's second-advent," and outwardly manifested the same by what he was, said, and did-so should we individually judge the proximity of our Lord's return. As our Law is unchanging and no respecter of persons-let us like WISE believe in simplicity for, and grow up to, that feast of good things-perhaps now out of reach-upon the top-shelf of the pantry, in our Father's house.-Jhn.14:1-3; 2 Cor.5:1-5.

SWORD-OF-THE-SPIRIT REVELATION 

169 

THE DIVIDING OF THE SWORD OF THE SPIRIT 

IN the "Triple Alliance," as set forth in the accom- Sanctuary, the body and the mind are typed as one, while the spirit is a consideration, standing apart-as More Holy than the other two or Most Holy of the three. 

It is so arranged for our instruction. The middle-cube is plainly the "bone of contention." Here we have a negative and a positive pole: a world-force on either side (Jhn.18:36; Gal.5:17), the body, or the present world, having the greater attraction. But the Law has, at least, made a "division in the camp". . . a splendid showing for a beginning!

This diagram pictures the "incompleted Christian-life of holiness or spirituality" and, it is clearly seen to be a decided improvement upon the sinful-past: all three cubes-snug together, operating in hilarious harmony. 

But the grace of God, upon the sixth-day of charactercreation, reverses the situation, and the body, which has been used to mould character, is discarded (Rev.12:9). 

Like the butterfly chrysilis-the "human nature" is cast out and the law becomes void. Then the "spirit takes its prey." In the world, but not of it (Jhn.15:19); absent from the body, and present with the Lord.-2 Cor.5:8.

There are numerous reasons why the mind of the natural-man favors the lower-nature. But tragic is the fact that millions of people are carnal minded, and live exclusively for the gratification of the senses, to the utter neglect of their real or spiritual selves. Such, it seems, should have been better off had they never been born-for they are missing the "true-purpose of life." 

Their characters are symbolized by the "unclean creatures referred to in Lev.11; Deu.14." Wisdom's laws will elevate a man to the "extreme opposite in character." 

This state-of-affairs can only be remedied by "breaking the will of the flesh," viz., killing the first-born or soul that-sinneth, repenting, and passing out from under the law into regenerating grace . . . with a complete change of mind or new set-of-faculties. This gives the intellect ascendency over the brute, imparts a thirst for knowledge of the truth and progress, and to do good instead of evil.

The second step in God's scheme of redemption is "to bring the Christian's reason into subjection to his conscience" via appointing the Holy Ghost as his teacher, instead of reactive-law (Jhn.16:13-15; Gal.3:24-26); which constitutes the blessing of sanctification. The old man's crucifixion is quite the contrary to pardoning a convict out of the penitentiary, for though the naturalman is physically free-his heart is unchanged, and the criminal nature may reassert itself, and put him back in prison, with another crime to his account.-Rom.7: 14-15. 

The Christian must do things reasonably, of course; but principle, and conscience are even more reliable guides. Reason is a balance, upon which we weigh one thing, "which we think we know," over against another of the same kind. Our minds should be trained to work in harmony with the conscience or "spirit balance." 

Final consequences reward and punishment stronger arguments for doing or not-doing things, than material gain or physical pleasure. are The reason of the carnal-minded Christian is often unscrupulous, like a rubber-band which can be stretched to fit the occasion (Jas.4:4,8). It usually excuses him by saying that, "This is an exceptional circumstance, and the thought, word, or deed was unavoidable, or entirely justifiable, at least pardonable!" And many times in the light of reason, alone he did follow the "right course." But, if he were a truly sanctified and sincerely conscientious Christian, how different his decision might have been! 

A man without a "good conscience" cannot be trusted.

Dual-minded Christians make poor martyrs, as they generally recant their principles-in a "pinch" or crisison the grounds that life is of far greater value than truth, character, or destiny. They shun the valley-ofhumiliation as disgraceful; and brand Christian-sacrifice, causing persecution or loss of reputation, as fanaticism or extremism. They, being neutral, are passive . . showing favor to either flesh or spirit, as convenient. 

But when gratitude to God-for benefits received, or love for his fellow men-impells the Christian to make a full-consecration, viz., place "his all" upon the altar and, thereafter make "daily sacrifices of self-denial:" die daily (as revelation directs) to material interests—

SWORD-OF-THE-SPIRIT REVELATION 

171 he thereby becomes a living-martyr; and God will sanctify or seal him with his Holy Spirit, ordaining him a vessel-unto-honor in his service.

As the veil-of-the-Temple was rent in twain, when Christ yielded up the ghost on Calvary's cross (Jhn.12: 

31; 19:30), and the inner oracle of the Most Holy was exposed, so, too, upon the sixth-day of character-creation, when the Christian has finished his body-sacrifice, his mind draws apart from the body or is spiritualized, and divine-love is perfected, and he enters into his rest the Mystery is Finished. Isa.62:6-7; Heb.4:10; ... Rev.10:7.
