Moreover, there is a constructive and destructive use of denial, suggestion, and affirmation. This mystic three-way stoniness of willpower, self-mastery, or "so-called Christian Science" is in the mind, as God's terrifying electric thunderbolts in the stormy-dome of the skies. Man is a GOD in the realm of his own affairs-in the willing of conscientious-reason the master of sensual impulse; but he becomes a devil in the hell of "antichrist upon the throne!"

Contrast the following scriptures speaking for and against "self assertion:" Deu.30:15,19; Job 2:10; 38:10-12; Isa. 45:7; Lk.9:23; Rom.6: 

11; 7:9-11; Eph.4:26-27; Jas.4:7; Rev. 20:7-10,-vs.- Psa.73:4-17; Ecl.7:

Huns or barbarians type reformation fears-because they exercise freedom of conscience. It is only martyr-spirited saints and totally lawless, irreverent or unbiased heathen, whose consciences-for being in balance or unseared (without the Mark of the Beast), can accurately discern or weigh sacred truths. The former are "wise to" and the latter "ignorantly innocent-of" all Antichrist self-deception and duplicity. These two extreme or exempt classes of persons-who are their natural or original selves-dare to do their own thinking; they fear God. but not man. Their innate sincerity abhors every lie-refuge: semblance of judgment-cheating or balance-tampering.— 

Ecl.7:29; Isa. 28:14-22.

Barbarians-for not having, intentionally or otherwise, come in contact with false-christ Christianity or man-of-sin authority-are a "law unto themselves" (Rom.2:14-16). Throughout the prophecy of scripture, God makes specific mention of these uncorrupted, therefore responsive characters, as his allies-who shall destroy the Babylon of Christianity; and so render possible the Second Reformation. Though they themselves are shamelessly at fault-they quickly detect and exceedingly loathe, a hypocritical profession of religion. 

Attila, king of the Huns, should probably have destroyed Rome but for being typically "bluffed" by the bugaboo authority of the Pope. He listened-like many another conqueror-and failed to fulfill his sacred mission.

20. PSYCHOLOGY OF A FEW MISUNDERSTOOD SCRIPTURE TEXTS 

Then Satan answered the Lord, and said, From going to and fro in the earth, and from walking up and down in it (Job 1:7; 2:2). Magnetic sensation-God's law in our members (Rom.7:5, 23; Jas. 4:1), or body-of-earth (Gen.3:19)-operates first in the thinking reason-making comparisons-or contrasting good over against evil (Deu.30:15, 19; 

32:31). Thought must always precede action (Pro. 23:7). When the decision is arrived at, the current of animal magnetism, or physical energy, is next directed to that part of the body, through the medium of the nervous system, which executes the deed and so circulates up and down, in response to the dictation of the will. 

Evidently, it was the carnal mind-personified in certain deceased justified "Christians" or sons of God (Jhn.1:12; Rom. 8:7, 14; Heb.4:11) -which disputed the perfection of Job (Heb.3:19: Rev. 12:10); and they, as one man or spirit (Judg. 20:8; Ezr.3:1; Phil.1:27); were the agents of this conspiracy, and Patient's good fortune (Job 1:6; 2:1; 

42:10; Psa.76:10; Jas.5:11; Jude 4).

Then was Jesus led up of the Spirit into the Wilderness (typing halfheartedness of the confused foolish virgin, Eze. 20:35-38), to be tempted of the devil (Mat.4:1). This incident has a mental setting. Our Saviour being human as well as divine-was tempted in all points as we (Heb. 

2:18; 4:15)-and every man is tempted of his own lust (Jas.1:14; 1 Jhn. 

2:16). The language here used is symbolical-as there is no mountain in Palestine where one can view all the kingdoms of the world (Ver.8) -excepting the mountain of Scriptural-evidence confirming Christ's Messiahship, for all Jewish prophecy points to him; and it assured him that Christianity should ultimately belt the globe (Dan.2:44, 45; 

Isa. 52:7; Mat. 24:14; 28:18; Phil.2:9-11). 

Resist the devil (desire of evil, Gen.6:5; Isa.59:19), and he (personified in you), will flee from you (Jas. 4:5,7). Two thoughts or things cannot occupy the same space or mind at once (Mat.6:24). Truth is stronger than error (Jhn.8:32), and light than darkness; a corresponding resolution back of these is irresistible (Mk.9:23). To conquer a bad habitdon't think about it-resent the thought of it determinedly, as you would death (Gen.2:17; Eph.4:27). Man has a free moral agency or prerogative-he can yield the balance of his will power to either the flesh or the spirit (Jos.24:15; Psa.2:6-11; Gal.5:17).

The reason the devil or desire-of-evil is so predominate in humanity-according to the scripture account-is that our first parents propagated their offspring on the forbidden youthful or foolish pleasureseeking side of life's meridian, rather than, like Abraham and Sarah's begetting of Isaac-the promised seed of overcoming-on the physical-declining and spiritual-inclined side of life; and as Christ, our Example, was born of a virgin, in a mystic or lustless unconscious conception, as females of animals have been bred without contact of the sexes, via injections, so symbolizing the new-creature birth of Christian regeneration, begotten in an acceptance of the pregnating truth of the word-of-God.

Your adversary the devil, as a roaring lion, walketh about, seeking whom he may devour (1 Pet.5:8). Man's flesh, or physical life, lusteth against the spirit, which is himself (Gal.4:29; 5:17; 1 Pet.2:11); and desires that which is evil continuously (Gen. 6:5; Mat.24:37). The desire of evil shifts about from one of the five senses to another-and the different organs of anatomy are alternately affected. God's law is supreme in authority, like a roaring lion (Rom. 8:2-8; Rev.13:2), which is king of beasts (Pro.19:12; 20:2; 30:30). If God's law can but convince a man that he is a sinner; if he even doubts his salvation, after repentance (Rom.14:23; Rev.12:4), he immediately becomes its prey, and is there and then devoured (Psa.22:21; Rom.7:7-14; Rev.17:12-14), or enters the belly of leviathan (Rom.2:9; 3:10-20)-this is the first death (Rom.7:11). God through his prophet Ezekiel promised to explain his law to us (Eze.29:3-6; 32:2, 4). To-day this prophecy is fulfilled-to the utter confusion and dismay of those who have been making merchandise out of the unsuspecting multitudes (Eze.32:9-16).

223 24. And in her was (imaged) emblemized the spirit of prophets, and of saints, and of all that were martyred reforming the Church (Mat.23:29-36; Rev.17:6). 

-: PERSONAL COMMENT : 

In our true spiritual-worship of God, we consistently expect a profit of good, better, or best results, just as we do in business or any other line of endeavor. Soul-food should be wholesome and nourishing, not indigestible or of a sawdust quality. Carefully consider the "illusiveliteralism or disappointing formalistic-churchianity" depicted in the foregoing_chapter of Christian Revelation. Then ask yourself, if it is any wonder that Christ disowns this apostate-part of his Church. 

How, possibly, could he countenance, much less commend, the type of piety here described? He plainly gives it his mid-week, foolish-virgin "stamp of disapproval." The confusion of such church-members is accounted for, by their not having advanced far enough spiritually to see themselves as Christ does; just as young peoples' conceit often prompts them to sneer at the preferences of their more experienced or better-informed elders.

A Visible Head of the Church and all the advantageous collection of worldly or material possessions, with which he naturally surrounds himself-in order to favorably impress unbelievers of his superiority, and so increase his prestige-is anything but vital or representive Christlikeness, as compared with the Philadelphian sixth-day Biblecommended perfection of perfect-love. Holy Numbers are certainly eye-openers, though abhorred by the non-progressive, to whom ignorance is bliss, or an excuse for their neglect.

Why should this Full Gospel Message of God's generosity or greatlove cause resentment? Let each individual Christian accept as much salvation as he wants, or eat of "proffered bounty" to his fill; but not condemn or hate his brethren, who have a more insatiable appetite for spiritual-truth.

What is now orthodoxy was formerly heresy; and acceptance was punishable with death. It would be appalling if there were no such thing as spiritual-progress; if all the Church need be indisposed because a part is so inclined!

The Christian Life is Witnessed with Stimulative Blessings. Self-Conquest is the Justification of the Saints. God Commends this Interpretation of 

His Word, Foretells the Benefits of its Study, and Final Triumph in Papacy's Overthrow.

THE FOUNDATION GEMS OF THE NEW JERUSALEM 

ODERN encyclopedias inform us that science has discovered a process of manufacturing artificial sapphire-gems. And we should naturally conclude that this reveals the mystery of the "character jewels" which they symbolize (Mal.3:17; Rev.21:18-20). And accounts for the necessity of subjecting the elect to chastisement or tribulation under a fiery-law (Deu.33:2; 1 Pet.1:7; 

2:5; 4:12); to the end that their earthen-vessels might be filled with the "true-riches or spiritual-treasure."- 2 Cor.4:7. 

Sapphires are made by the crystalization of the earth alumina (as typing the transformation or resurrection of our earthy bodies, 1 Cor.15:42-50), the characterizing ingredient of common-clay (humanity, Job 4:19; 10:9; 

13:12; 33:6), variously colored with the right proportion of a metalic oxide or salt (typing faith, Mat.5:13; 

Mk.9:49-50; Rom.1:17; 12:3-6; Heb.11:6); all shades of which are the product of a single base (typing the Word of promise made flesh, Jhn. 1:12,14; Rom.10:17; 2 Cor. 3:3). 

The aluminum (typing the good or God in us all, Ex. 

7:5, 11:7; Mat.19:17; Jhn.10:34-37) is first separated from its silicate (typing sin, Gen.43:32; Ex.8:22-23; 

9:25-26; Rom.7:16-25), by fusion with a base (representing the dividing-law or sword-of-the-Spirit, Ex.1: 

8-14; Jhn.15:3; Heb.4:12), having a stronger affinity for silicic-acid than aluminum.

The first stage in the process of manipulation is to mix equal parts of porcelain-clay and red-lead in a crucible (Isa.1:18; Rom.9:17-23; Gal.5:17-21), enclosing this in a second crucible; and exposing them all for several weeks to an intense heat in a crystal-furnace (Deu. 

4:20; Dan.3:19-25; 4:16-25; 9:24,27; Heb.12:5-12).

Two crucibles are necessary (typing a mind in the body, Mat.25:41; Mk.15:22; Rom.8:7; 12:2; Eph.4:23), as the red-lead (typing the law-of-sin, Isa.1:18; Rev. 

6:4; 12:3-4) combines with the silicia of the inner one, and eats holes in it (typing exposure and condemnation of unrighteousness, Isa.1:6; 64:6; Hab.3:13; Mat.13:33; 

Rom.7:18,25; Phil.3:3).

FOUNDATION JEWELS 

231 Upon allowing the crucibles to cool, and breaking the inner one (1 Pet.1:9-13; Rev.11:19), two strata of mineral are in evidence: an upper or glassy one (Rev.4:6; 

15:2), and a lower one containing clusters of carborundum crystals (typing the lowly-graces of godliness, Psa. 

37:11; Pro.1:9; 4:9; Mat.23:12; Rev.21:18-20).

Color diversifies the stones (purposes are revealed in marked-service, Acts 10:4, 15,22; 22:12). Transparency types shamelessness (Jol.2:26; Rom.1:16; Rev.21:25). 

Shape types conduct (Mat.7:16-22). And hardness types steadfastness (Acts 4:11-13; 1 Cor.15:58; 2 Tim.2:3). Alumina in its pure natural state is the mineralsapphire (Isa.51:1). Nothing but a furnace will work out the "beauties or wealth-of-purification" (Rom.8:1823; 1 Cor.3:13; 2 Cor.4:10-11,16-18).

GOD'S THE GATES OF PEARL 

OD'S promise to king David, that "he should always have a light burning in Is-real, or a son sitting upon his throne" was, spiritually speaking, made good in Christ. By rejecting him, as preferring a temporal to a spiritual kingdom, the Jews forfeited what their oracle of Urim and Thummin stood for (Ex.28: 

21,30; Jhn.8:31-32), which was composed of twelve precious "speaking stones," the emblems of godliness, characteristic of the various blessings of the sons of Jacob, and subsequently of the twelve tribes. The right of inheritance consequently descended to spiritual-Isreal, consisting of both Jews and Gentiles (Jhn.10:16; Rom. 

2:28-29; 9:6-8): the twelve apostles of Christ replacing the patriarchs of the Old Dispensation. And types and shadows were thus merged into the true spiritual worship of God.-Jhn.4:23-24.

Those Hebrews who still held to the strict observance of the Mosaic law (Mat.19:20), were, by the breaking of the brotherhood between Judah and Israel (Zech.11: 

14), henceforth classified as denizens of spiritual-Judah, under the law (Isa.19:17; Rom.3:19; Gal.3:10). And, verily, they receive their reward for walking in such light as they have (Mat.6:2,5,16); though the crowning results of law and grace are vastly different, as a comparison of pearls and sapphires reveals.-Rev.21:10-21.

Science informs us that pearls are formed by means of a parasite, or some foreign substance, finding lodgment within the shell or flesh of the mother-of-pearl oyster. This produces an acute inflammation and the mollusk, being unable to dislodge the adversary-in self defense it throws out a life-liquid or animal secretionof-resistance; and thereby literally overcomes or wallsin the intruder. What a perfect type is this, of the manner in which the Christian must overcome trials!-1 Cor. 

10:13; Phil.2:12-13.

The name carbuncles (boils), ascribed to pearls in Isa. 

54:12, suggests quite another cause, as denoting a bruise or diseased condition of the system (Isa.1:6; 53:10).

Though this may refer to general appearances only. In any case, the pain suffered would be intense; as attested by that quite-common experience in human life . . . the swelling of a boil. A very unpleasant thing to even contemplate, as also the chastening of the Lord (Deu.32: 

39; Hos.6:1; Heb.12:12). Job, the perfect man, had numerous boils (typing trials), but not one to spare,as evinced by the end of his affliction, which amply justified the means of his enrichment (Job 42:12; Jas.1: 

2-4; Rev.2:9). He consistently asked, "Shall we receive good at the hand of God, and shall we not receive evil?"

Both of these providences are "equally perfecting-considerations of a loving Father" (Isa.45:7; Jas.1:17). Yet sometimes it requires exhausting-faith to "thankfully accept" God's disguised-blessings!

Study the 29th. and 30th. chapters of Genesis: the petty annoyances of Jacob's family life. His domestic affairs are typical of wrestlings which enrich every Christian experience with virtues "thus born into existence." Children are said to be the riches of the poor. 

Whatsoever good or bad there is in parents, their offspring will help to work it out, be assured! 

The Law doesn't send peace into the home, he sends a sword; the Word divides (Mat.10:34). To prove his great love, God chastens us (Rev.3:19). Man is born unto trouble as the sparks fly upward (Job 5:7; Psa. 

119:67,71), viz., fiery trials direct our attention to the need of higher-ideals or spiritual-considerations. Truly, affliction is indispensable to both enlightenment and progress.

Pearls are formed of carbonate of lime, similar to the water-sediment of the kitchen teakettle, interstratified

GATES OF PEARL REVELATION 

233 with animal-membrane of self-denial. The history of severe trial and a mighty-struggle, against extreme odds -is unmistakably and wonderously recorded in their construction. There is an alternating layer of selfsacrifice and resultant compensation; the carbonaceousstrata lending character and durability to the gem; while the color and brilliancy is imparted by the expended-resistance of a distinctly animal-fluid, otherwise unnoticed. A combination of these two elements being essential to "true value" or inviting-beauty. When we behold either literal or spiritual pearls, we should consider their source! ...

So it is with either Jew or Gentile who, in the light of a good-conscience, is doing his duty toward God and man they obtain merit thereby. Works of the law justify no man, but faith exercised, either under-thelaw or under-grace, is effective in the "very nature-ofthings."

Our Law, in defining a man of shrewd business ability, states that he seeks until he locates-then he buys a pearl of great-price, viz., all of Christlikeness or Christianity is "a transaction which, invariably, leaves self a bankrupt." A sinner must surrender his 

All, and a Christian consecrate his All-in order to duplicate the Master's promised virtue-of-stead (stand) fastness of the cross.-Mat.13:46; 1 Pet.2:21; Rev.2:17.

While our Law allots first place to sapphires (foundation stones, Gal.2:9); and secondly to pearls-in his kingdom-what would a walled-city represent without gates, which are indicative of "intercourse with the whole outside-world?" Therefore carefully consider the "symbolic-significance of the pearl."

A Transformed Mind and a Redeemed Body are the Credentials of Full Fellowship in the Church or Bride of Christ, of which a City is an Ideal Type, as the Preferences of its Citizens

have Settled Them where its Founder Lived.

TWO FIRING ROLL LECTURES The "First Witness" Reminiscently Appears Second.

FLYING-ROLL DESCRIPTION 

243 

INTRODUCTORY FLYING ROLL DESCRIPTION THE 

HE name "Flying Roll" signifies "progressive light or life;" and no light, like experience, opens blind eyes and rectifies mistakes. The A to Z of Christianity or the whole counsel of God is testified to as inside, personal, or experimental information in this model F. R. or visibilized first-witness of Christlikeness; and nothing short of a "typified experience" can excell the F. R. interpretation of the hidden mystery-then, of course, the spirit-of-the-Word is preponderately more blessed than its dead-letter body (2 Cor.3:9). The unsealing of this instruction is evidence of a genuine unselfish or loving-spiritual communism of the willing WISE.

This F. R. consists of a single-sheet of paper, ten by twenty inches, printed upon both sides. Each linealinch represents an eighteen-times-its-length cubit of the original visualized-pattern. Yet nothing symbolical has been sacrificed by scaling down the F. R. to manipulative size; for its larger dimensions only have a relative value, or refer to the porch entrance of the house of Solomon's temple. This "threshold emblem" represents a reasoning person's vestibule or porch observation of the two mystic installments of holiness. 

The second F. R. flight convinces the compromising- 

Christian that he is robbing his spirit of the imparted 

Most Holy blessing (Mal.3:8-10). The first F. R. flight proves to the nominal-Christian, that he is a hypocrite without the blessing of imputed-holiness. 

Solomon's porch intermediates the holies and the Altar of the Inner Court. Here one can judge the advisability of making the required sacrifices for one or both commended blessings. The term "cub-its" denotes transforming-humiliations or cringing-approaches of the lower or sacrificed bestial-nature, before the coveted super-man or Christ-observed in the worshipped or exemplary Master.

The F. R. is folded into six equal sections or wings; and when radiating light, these wings are lifted and lowered, as a bird flies. They represent nights or mystery-curtains, dividing the week of character-creation into days or revelations.

The sabbath or seventh day-of-rest is indicated, by closing-up and laying-aside the F.R.; as there is nothing more to be hoped for, except steadfastness and service, when overcoming is finished. Notice carefully, the night

or trial-of-faith is always foremost in the F.R., and bars the way to the conditional light or revelation. All of the retrospection of a finished character-creation is portrayed upon the backside or rear-cover of the F. R.; and all of its prospect appears upon the front-cover. "Watching" the miracle of a wicked thinking, speaking, and acting sinner (if we but confess the truth) made wholly Christlike why should we expect any other "resurrection of our bodies," than that typed between the F. R. covers (Eph.2:1-7; Phil.3:21)?

What a repelling arrayal of soul-proving lamentation (hell), mourning (death), and woe (regret), is involved in every self-conquest, and is inclusive of the F. R. contents! But that soon becomes past-experience, and is discounted in the satisfaction of counscious-Christlikeness, which is the immortal-treasure for which we all journeyed to these planet shores from ... God knows where; without which it seems it would be better for the dying, never to have been born.

A glance at the diagram, upon the rear-cover of the F. R., yields the sum-total of cross-bearing, walking in the footsteps of Christ, or the fellowship of his sufferings. Seven is conceded to be the perfect-number of scripture; and the purpose of the world's creation is seen in the finished-mystery of character-creation, as prophesied in Dan.12:4,9; Rev.10:7.

This "book" of prophecy, written within and summarized upon the backside, is understood in the breaking of its seven seals, or in the transition-degrees of spiritual-progress. Christ warns those who love his appearing to "watch," lest he come suddenly and unexpectedly, as a thief in the night. God's "watch" has seven numerals upon its dial to denote the passing of time. Too, we should mark well the Events of each week-day. In seeking to be perfect, we will study the science of these time-signs of spiritual-multiplication, and run patiently to the time-of-the-end goal, where our faithfulness will be amply rewarded with a crown of everlasting-life.

Christ's literal-return to the earth, would cause a gathering to him of his followers from every quarter of the globe. It is utterly absurd to think that they should thus get as thoroughly acquainted with him, as though they should, instead, seek and obtain a "reproduction of his character," as outlined in the F. R. Neither could

FLYING-ROLL DESCRIPTION 

245 they please him as well, nor assist him in his ministry as much, by being physically-rather than spirituallynear him. Let us obtain the all-important inner secondadvent of Christ; and there won't be any hankering for a questionable literal-reappearance. 

The F. R. folder, in its two flights, outlines both halves of the at-one-ment, or imputed Beauty and imparted Bands, viz., heirship and its conditional-induce ments (Rom.8:17-19). The first X XX 12X of the 

XXXXXXX, denotes justification-of-regeneration in repentance; the second infers sanctification-of-singlemindedness, via consecration, with its understanding and ministery qualification.

These experiences imply a twofold parelleling of the will (2X) in submission to God, or death of the sinfulnature, and crucifixion of the carnal-nature. The first gives Egypt's emancipated-sinners the Title of Christians or an objective Promised Land. And the second, which consists of a pair of blessings-or includes the one preceding-grants them Possession, via gradual conquest, of a Holy (body) Land. 

The Lord prophesied through Zechariah, that he should break his staff-of-discipline in two, even Beauty. 

And although both divisions are portrayed in the F. R., you can only obtain them singly, no matter how great your zeal, anxiety, or faith. Your new-creature birth is first witnessed; and then your maturity or old-man crucified, via consecration or all on the altar-which stands for the "earnest or balance" of your inheritanceof-Christlikeness.

Emphatically, it is not merely the art-work and blended-colors of the F. R. that makes it a masterpiece of 

Beauty... it's the treasured-blessings commended, and the analyzed experiences of overcoming, revealed and illustrated in its map-like unfolding, that appeals. 

The thief or dual Christian, in his compromise with the flesh, is plainly discounting his salvation 50%; and his boasted character-building is only a foundation. He is dividing and subtracting his inner Christ, spirit or better-self. He so admits that the outer Holy Place of the common priesthood is more fascinating to him than the inner Most Holy, typing a whole hearted highpriest's character. And the nominal-Christian or hypocrite must confess that a preliminary Solomon's porch or vestibule threshold-experience, of merely looking into 

---

or investigating his inherited-holiness, is all that he wants, and is familiar with, and so can explain. 

The Lord prophesied that he would popularize the F. R., and so prove to the double-minded that he hasn't exercised faith for "the equivalent of a second flight, or condemning-manipulation of its wings," otherwise his mind should now be single. And that the hypocrite or false-professor of Christlikeness, never repented with a Jonah-like sorrow for disobedience, as portrayed by its analytic first-flight of wings, otherwise he would not now be shamming Christianity. It, too, will be impossible for either of them to ignore this antichrist-detector's outline of their experiences; because its truths are so convincingly set forth.

The mystic-timber, consumed in the F. R. consideration, infers any and all absence of spiritual-growth and edifying, assuring testimony-of-progress (Psa.84:7; Pro. 

4:18; Isa.61:3; 65:22). Stones denote confirmatoryfoundation and defensive-walls of overcoming virtue and grace (Isa.26:1; 54:11-12; Rev.21:18-21), which are barricades against temptation, trials, and opposing foes. 

Of course, the F. R. is also intended to be a source of stimulation and consolation, in retrospect, to the possessor of its prefigured blessings; and he will cherish it as a reliable reference-chart, of what he has been saved from and to; and as safe-directions for others, who seek to him for advice.

This portable, folding or collapsible, tent-like F. R. is the emigrating Christian's map of life's pathway; and fittingly substitutes for the O. T. Wilderness Tabernacle, which was also used in Canaan up to the time when king Solomon finished his temple-of-destiny upon Mt. 

Moriah; toward which all sojourning Hebrew-worshipers, while it existed, turned their faces in prayer. Thus the 

F. R. will ever be venerated by the Is-real seekers after the true-God, until they are certain that the days of their pilgrimage are over. 

On the backside of the F. R., in a flash of omniscience, antichrists are timely diverted from seeking the reardoor of a store to buy an Eveready or dependable flashlight, viz., there is no crossless short-cut or round-about way to overcoming. Besides, the leviathan, under-thelaw lamp of first-witness or sign-of-the-prophet-Jonas, automatically becomes the desired Eveready, with a changed-heart or battery-renewal . . . indulgences, de-

FLYING-ROLL DESCRIPTION 

247 nials, affirmations, and suggestions to the contrary, or other lie-refuges. Antichrist has various subterfuges and substitutes for legal front-door keys; all to no avail. 

It is impossible to receive any evidence of a second blessing, without an experimental-knowledge of the first. 

Man's will, naturally athwart or in disagreement with God's will, must be "twofold Lawfully altered" perpendicular to the divine-plan. Wise overcomers will, as 

Christ exemplified, shorten the ordeal, or exchange the "cross of duty"-misfitting and chafing rebellion-for preferencial harmony.

The time-of-the-end leviathan and Eveready flashlights of the F. R. are symbolic of the foolish and wise virgins' illuminating - experiences and resultant-testimonies. Their bodies, as mystic sanctuaries for the worship of God, are typed by this mechanical-device of prophecy. Isaiah 62:1 reads: For "Signs' sake" will I not hold my peace, and for "Jesus-ruse-ails-them" sake I will not rest, until the righteousness thereof go forth as brightness, and the salvation thereof as a lamp that burneth. Here you see this prophecy's fulfillment; and the lamp is described, in detail, in Job 41. The Christian's body is electrically magnetized or generated with transient-life, precisely as is this lamp; make close comparisons and be convinced. Human bodies, like 

Jonah's whale, are prison-houses until we lead captivity captive, via reformation unto transformation; which is witnessed by the Holy Ghost-as we switch on the light of this lamp.

Conclusively, when our moon-like reflection finally favors the sunlike spirit, resident in the circulated-blood of our flesh, as restraining the blind-lusts, serpentineor-crawling currents of the nervous-system; and our consciences are ashamed of the company which we are obliged to keep, of our adversary-the dual or physical life the four living or spiritual creatures of the fourfold-gospel, then become apparent in their serviceable time-of-the-end sacrifices of wonder, and we behold the Word repersonified or made flesh in his second-coming. 

Christ is the invisible spiritual-Head of the body or Church and we, exemplarily and serviceably, are the flesh and bone members thereof.

The good, better, and best self, dormant in mankind, is the hidden-mystery of scripture, prefigured by the twice manipulated wings of this time-of-the-end F. R.

BROKEN “BANDS" OR MYSTERY-SEALS 

We, in the rough or crude, are, at best, but "valuablematerial which needs to be worked up" by the master Builder; but as free-agents, all material Must submit. It is the far and foresighted who become joyfully passive; the near-sighted or materialistic are unconcerned and rebellious. Admittedly, it goes against nature. . . to be chopped down, sawed up, seasoned, plained down, bored into, chiseled, sand-papered off, etc.; but we are making our appeal to your conscience and reason-not to your perverted-senses, which must "disciplinarily suffer" in the transformation. As we value clothes, the wise build characters precautionarily, to avoid future - shame (2 Cor.5:1-4). 

The last and best part of our testimony is "the end well justifies the means!" and no apparel ever fit or became you near so well, or afforded so much satisfaction, yet was so reasonable in price-as is Christlikeness.

IT 

CHRIST'S IMAGE IN THE MAKING, or the Sevenfold Influence of Truth or Light upon the Sensitive Conscience of Man, as Relating to Character Building, and Illustrated by the Flying Roll and the Art of Photography. 

is a well known fact, that the "original photographgallery" was the human-skull. And the camera was patterned after those eyes or windows-of-the-soul, which let in light through the sense-of-sight; and God was the inventor (Isa.48:2-11). Do not express surprise, therefore, that he should make use of these as symbols. 

A "finished-photograph," of one's self or a friend, is usually a cherished - possession. But that wonderful science, which makes possible such treasures, also comes in for a share of commendation. Yet, who would have ever imagined that photography had anything in common with the "divine-science of redemption?" But you have probably noticed that the Bible often refers to images and imaginations, which shape themselves upon the walls of the mind. And we think by a consideration of these same images or pictures-such as the imageof-God, evil imaginations-of-the-heart, the image-of- 

Christ, Christ the express-image of the Father, imaginations as strongholds, the image-of-the-beast, etc. 

Mental-images and their worship are either constructive or destructive to character-building for, as a man thinketh in his heart, the seat-of-affection, so is he (Pro. 

23:7). Verily, a lustful or murderous thought is procreator of the deed (Mat.5:28; 1 Jhn.3:15). God searcheth the hearts (preferences, Psa. 7:9; Rev. 2:23) and reins (principles); for he judges thereby. 

Conscience is God's oracle. Conscientious-reasoning implies intuition or consultation with the Holy Ghost or guardian-angel, regarding these images. How diabolically sacrilegious to stifle, as with a hot-iron, the stillsmall voice of condemnation! That is the Mark of the

Beast, as it favors bestiality, and begets "confusion of judgment" (1 Tim.4:2; Tit.1:15; Rev.13:17).

A portrait has value, only as it reminds us of the living-personality back of it. So, too, the science of spiritual-photography plays its part in temple or character building by affording a "pattern to work by."

The greeting, appearing upon the front-cover of the F. R. indicates its contents, also that, in the beginning, there is in mind no spiritual-light or knowledge of the 

Christ-the Sun of righteousness (Mal.4:2; Jhn.8:12)who condemns a man or reveals to him his good, better, and best self (Gen.1:2; Jer.4:22-23; Eph.5:13). He is shut out of doors (Rev.3:20).

The F. R., so far as understanding goes, is then but a blank, unfolded sheet-of-paper. The negative and positive plates of spiritual photography-answering to a good-conscience-bear no image; and the mystery is a "great deep." But light enters the mind, via contact with the spoken or written word (Rom.4:15; 10:14-18; 

2 Cor.3:3); this produces a sense-of-guilt, causing fear (2 Cor.5:10; Heb.10:27), which apprehends, convicts, and shuts the sinner up, under-the-law (Rom.3:19-20; 

13:1-7). And this effects the sensitive-conscience, precisely as light does the photograph plate or film; and leaves a negative-impression upon the mind. The result is a portrait or image, in mind, of an unjust-judge who sentences sinners to be punished. simply because they do not want to be unnaturally holy and peculiar. If you were to consider our Saviour, by "that negative-portrait," you would conclude that he was a tyrant. True to the character of negatives-black appears for white, and vice versa. This is making Christ of (ill) no reputation. But it is character, not reputation, which our Creator is concerned about (1 Sam.16: 

7). So watch prayerfully the transforming process. 

Now perhaps you would like an introduction to the plan of the F. R. photograph-studio? The principle part of a photographer's equipment, of course, is his camera and dark-room fixtures. The front-cover of the F. R. primarily represents the door of his studio, secondarily his camera. The circular-aperture, in the center, types the focusing-lens used to cut the coveted-image upon the sensitized-plate within, when the shutter is lifted and the exposure is made.

Neither the photographer nor his camera are respecters of persons. Everybody receives a glad welcome. That is the "outstanding feature" of this business (Jhn.3:16; 6:37)!

The second-wing of the F. R. types the negative, foundation or fundamental plate or film used in portraitmaking. The third-wing represents the positive plate or

film; which is a reversal of the former. Though a secondary-consideration, it is as indispensable as the superstructure-of-a-house, which graces its foundation. A positive image cannot be made without a negative. And the latter, though invaluable, is found woefully wanting, without its mate. A man who lays a foundation, and does not erect a house upon it, is justly mocked by the passing crowd (Lk.14:29).

Upon the first-page of the fourth-wing, behind which is the dark-room of the F. R., we see a symbol which dazzles our eyes; somewhat resembling a pile of glittering-coin. This is a rainbow-representation of the photographer's - compensation, which he ever keeps in mind, from the beginning to the finish of portrait making. When his "week's work" is done, business profit is that which seems to afford the greatest degree of satisfaction, and justify all his pains-taking toil, and the incidental expense of portrait-making. And when this is placed in his hand, he smiles and thanks you; and, at the same time, hands you your parcel of photos. 

Most of us know, by actual experience, that entering a photographer's shop, and posing before his camera, is a comparatively easy matter. In fact, it is quite like attending Church, and sitting still in a pew, while the preacher makes a target of you. But the door of the photographer's dark-room, behind which he develops and fixes his pictures, has not the least attraction for anybody. So he makes it more inviting by mentally hanging, just outside, some such symbolism as this, of the "recompense of his reward" (Heb.11:26); which makes it easier for him to say good-bye to the pleasantsurroundings outside; and endure, and perhaps partly forget, for the time being, his disagreeable-environment within.

This self-denial is an absolutely indispensable and unavoidable feature of his calling; but he figures that the end justifies the means. Nothing but "sufficient remuneration," however, could induce him to spend so much of his life in that dark, dismal place. Darkness has no such charms as light; but amid the terrors of night, are hidden the treasures, which buy the pleasures of light. 

Now, please enter with me into the dark-room, by "pulling down the curtain or fourth-wing of the F. R."

And take particular notice... this chamber is divided

into two sections, which type the "double treatment" to which plates, films, and prints are all subjected. 

The left-hand folded, or lower unfolded page, represents the photographer's developing-fluid. The folded right-hand, unfolded upper page, types his hypo-solution or fixing bath. For it is necessary that both the negatives and positives be developed with "strong chemicals," suspended in water, in order to bring out the obscure-image upon the sensitized-film. 

At first it doesn't seem possible that any picture should be there. But persistent immersion in the bath, is soon rewarded by a sure-enough portrait, visible to the naked-eye. But, what put the image there? Why, it was produced by a sharp, well-timed flash of lightlike a revelation (Lk.17:24)-passing through the accurately-adjusted lens, when the shutter of the camera was lifted, for just the fraction of a minute. And the delicate-surface of the plate was "so shocked or taken by surprise," that it simply had to remember what the person looked like, who was posing on the outside. 

And so it is, with your mind, when the preacher speaks, or the author writes the truth-in a convincing manner-convinction seizes upon you. And you believe the truth, because you are "effectually enlightened" (Acts 24:25). If Christ were not our Example or the

Light-of-the-world, his word would not make any "such impression" upon our minds (Heb.4:12). E.g., close your eyes and suddenly open them, you will believe what you see. But one pair-of-eyes does not see everything. So we must believe other people's eyes; and consider the imagery of many minds. We should have faith in God and his prophets (Lk.24:25). 

Of course, the photographer must exercise "professional judgment," and not allow the plates, etc. to remain in the developer too long or, too short a time, or they will be spoiled. Neither does our Lord, in character-creation, unduly expose us to temptation or trial (1 Cor.10:13). 

When the portraits are fully developed, they are transferred to the second-compartment, called the hypo or fixing bath, composed of a mixture-of-chemicals in solution, which fix or establish the images, so that they will be durable, and not fade away when exposed to the bright sunlight outside.

This wholly explains the details of the dark-roomwith the exception that every trace of any chemical must be finally rinsed off the image, with pure water; and the plates dried. Then the negatives are ready for printing purposes; and the positives are Eveready for mounting (1 Thes.4:17). And, so far as this batch is concerned, the trials of the dark-room and its "dreadful chemicals," are things of the past. If only they could speak, you would be thrilled with their testimonies-ofendurance and their expressions-of-joy, over their "improved condition."

Now, since you have been shown through the photograph-gallery of the F. R., and the process of portraitmaking has been explained to you-I wonder if, meanwhile, you have been able to reconcile the "art of photography" with God's plan of character-building? Anyhow, we shall, via illustration, suppose that you are a photographer. And that you are engaged to make a portrait of your best Friend-Jesus the Christ-so that the world may look upon it in admiration (2 Cor.3:3); fall in love with him; and give him its heart and hand. "Oh!" you exclaim, "that would be an impossibility, for Christ has been in the spirit-world for thousands of years, since his incarnation. So long, in fact, that people are weary of waiting for his return and, unless he should quickly come, I shall not see him."

My reply is "that is just what spiritual-photography or image-building is for-it is the only means by which we can materialize the Christ. We must look for him in ourselves" (Mat. 24:25-28; Gal. 4:19; Col. 1:26-28; 

1 Jhn.3:2,7; 4:17). Jesus will come in the "clouds of heaven" (Mat.24:30; Acts 1:11; 1 Cor.2:7; Rev.1:7), that is, through an understanding of the mysteries which "becloud or perplex your mind" (Mat.13:11-17); for he is the Word of God (godliness, Jhn.1:1; Rev.19: 

13). His appearance will be, as positive as his disappearance in that your flesh will be, his flesh; and your bones, his bones (1 Cor.12:27; Eph.5:30). The lightning-like "revelation of his coming" will be in a crisis of self-abandonment, when you have lost your identity in the at-one-ment of his cross (Gal.2:20; 5:24; Col. 

3:3-4); your no-self is Christ! 

Has all previous "watching" for our Lord's return been, but fruitless self-denial? Is this injunction to "watch," destined to be an "empty service" to all but

a single-generation of believers? Must we confess that even we, ourselves, are "almost certainly" watching to no purpose? Should any Christian be barred from the realization of this blessed hope? If so, what "special importance" can possibly be attached to the event? 

A spiritual-interpretation of the prophecy makes it open to all. A literal-interpretation predicts disappointment, as the award of the majority. Is our God any respecter of persons? Christ's first-advent was literalthat the dead-letter or firstfruits of the word might appear (Jhn.15:22); and that the law and the prophets should be exemplarily fulfilled (Mat.5:17; Lk.24:44; 

Jhn.3:19). But his second-advent, since his departure, has been, is, and shall be universally optional and spiritual (Jhn.14:19-20).

If Christians would only seek, in faith, a personal and spiritual fulfillment of this promise or prophecy (Jhn. 

4:23,24; 6:63; 2 Cor.3:6,8). If they would only believe and teach that, "spiritual events" were, and are, and will be, as true, and fully as important as literal, material or symbolical incidents ever could be the scoffers would soon be put to silence. And there would be no "seeming contradictions" in God's word.

A literal second-coming, at best, is an “uncertain prospect"-a mere speculation-to those who refuse to place a "spiritual construction" upon the word-of-promise. Why should we, like the fabled dog, with real-meat in his mouth, drop it and grab after a shadow (Col.2:17; 

2 Tim.4:4; Heb.10:1)? When the Master said that we should do greater works than he, the inference was that the exercise of our spiritual-gifts would excel the value of any material-blessing (Jhn.14:12). Where are those bodies which our Lord healed and raised from the dead?

On the other hand, if we win souls, and lead the blind or erring out of darkness into the light-of-truth, they are everlastingly benefitted. We discard our materialbodies at death; but character-which is the enduringgarment of the spirit-is ever with us (Eph.5:27; Rev. 

19:8). Christ is already among us "in spirit" (Jhn.14: 

3; 16:7). What would his visible-presence signify, in proportion to an "individual-revelation of his finishedimage" (Eph.4:13)? 

Without the law there is no transgression (Rom.4:15; 

5:13). But when a man hears or reads the word of God ... it is as though Christ, who is the Light-of-the-world

or the Word-of-God, vindicated in flesh, stood before him in person. His beautiful life, spotless character, and unblemished reputation-reveals to a man his verybest or perfect self. Which, compared with his fallencondition and undeveloped-character, produces a senseof-guilt, that effects his conscience—if he is honest with himself as light does the exposed-negative within the photographer's-camera.

When the plate is removed from the camera and taken into the dark-room, as indicated by "pulling-down the curtain" or fourth-wing of the F. R., the third-wing goes along with it. Otherwise the second-wing, which represents the negative, wouldn't be in the dark-room.

This also denotes the fact that "the photographer has its mate in mind, or he wouldn't trouble himself with the negative at all." It is immediately plunged into the developer (Psa.51:2; Isa.30:20; Jna.2:3), as typed by the rear-page of the fourth-wing of the F. R., which in spiritual-photography is called a "sorrow for sin" (Psa. 

116:3). This naturally follows a knowledge-of-sin or inward-look, typed by the F. R. window (Jas.1:25,27). At this critical stage we carefully "watch" for the materializing-mystery (Gen.1:7,9); but, Horrors! is this an image-of-the-Christ (Gen.1:10,12)? Yes, but it is a "prenatal misconception," produced by fear "contortion of Truth" (Mat.10:25). Otherwise, it would not be a perfect-negative (Psa.111:10). It is a lothsome, hideous, black lie: the likeness of a most "repulsive being" (Isa.53:1-3; Jhn.8:48). Prejudice distorted the features of the Christ. The operator, who focused the lens and timed the exposure, was dishonest; for all who know the Christ, confess his comliness. Yet this negative must be developed, for it represents the "foundation of spiritual-photography," or the first-installment of the plan of character-creation. 

When the sinner is sufficiently "sorry for his sins," he-his spirit-is then taken out of the developer, and placed into the hypo or fixing bath of repentance, which is symbolized by the front-page of the fifth-wing of the 

F. R. Without this "additional treatment" the imageon-the-negative will perish, fade away, or backslide 

(Lk.13:3; Acts 3:19). 

Repentance involves an unconditional-surrender of the will to God, a "facing right about," or death of the first-born character (1 Cor.15:46-50)-as Isaac became

heir to the promise, instead of Ishmael; as Jacob supplanted Esau, his elder brother; as Ephraim took Manasseh's blessing, as the death-angel slew all the firstborn of Egypt. Repentance "fixes the mind," so that it agrees to reason things out; and no longer be governed by instinct, or animal-sense (Isa.1:18).

When this is an accomplished-fact, it is needless to longer keep the transgressor in prison, as the law has reformed him (Deu.32:29-30; Gal.3:24-25). So he is instantly pardoned; which is equivalent to thoroughly washing all the chemicals off the photographer's negative, in pure water (Eze.36:25-27).

Cessation of condemnation, and a revelation of the new-birth (Jhn.3:3-6; 1 Pet.1:23),-occurs so suddenly, as "flinging back" the four upper-wings of the F. R.

This act lets us out of the dark-room. And now we shall consider what was accomplished therein. We see, upon the middle right-hand section, four burning wheels or lamps, which represent the testimony and influence of the four spiritual creatures-four in one (Eze.1:26; 10: 

14; 2 Cor.5:17). Below the spiritual-creatures is the key-of-faith, which switched the lights on (Eph.2:8; 

1 Jhn.5:4), and transformed the gloom-of-hell into the brightness-of-heaven (Isa.62:1; Eph.2:6). Further down is pictured leviathan, out of whose black-belly the sinner has just escaped (Jna.2:10). And over the heads of the cherubim-fourfold transition (Eze.10:1,19,21; Rev. 

4:3)-is the glory of the God-of-Israel. So here we have a portrayal of the past, present, and future state, of the new-born Christian, or babe in Christ-reading from the bottom up.

The four enlightening-wheels signify that every Christian has-in embryo-a fourfold ability and pesonality, obtained through successive generations or changes-ofmind (Isa.51:8), during the four days-of-grace, comprising Christian experience or life. Noah and his three sons; Job, the perfect-man, and his three friends; Daniel and his three associates; Christ and his three nearestdisciples . . . were antitypes of the spiritual-creatures. 

Each one, of each group, has a light, gift, or influence, differentiating him from the others (1 Cor.7:7; 12:4).

And each had to fill the office of the others, before he could become perfect, and understand the Finished 

Mystery (Mat.26:36-40; Rev.10:7).

What would you think of a photographer who offered you a negative for a finished-portrait; and insisted that it was a "perfect-likeness," plenty good enough; and would suit anybody, but a crank? I, already know what you would think and say; and just how you would feel and act, under such circumstances. 

Worse still,-suppose he were to hand you a blankplate, or a partly-developed negative, or one perfectlydeveloped, but not fixed; and he didn't dare to look at it, for fear that it had already faded away in the condemning-sunlight (Jhn.3:20)? As ridiculous and unbelievable as the assertion may seem, spiritually speaking, this is the discourtesy "popularly accorded Jesusthe-Christ," when he poses for his portrait (Zech.5:3; 

Mal.3:8-10). Please weigh this statement carefully; for the truth may come closer home than you, at first, are inclined to think.

Justified Christians seem to get tired or indifferent, after they have made a negative-portrait of Jesus. And go about, ignorantly misrepresenting his "imputed righteousness," which is obtained without works (Rom. 

4:1-11), for his "imparted righteousness," which is only secured by spiritually duplicating his sacrifice (2 Cor. 

4:10-11; Gal.2:20; 5:24). Thus the world is made ashamed of the Christ-image, and wants nothing to do with it (Jol.2:17; Rom.2:24). Such are indeed Christians... but they only have a "negative reason" for saying so. They profess to have salvation-but they are only "half saved." Personal-sins are canceled; but inbred sin remains (Heb.3:10-14). The word calls them thieves, because they are misappropriating for worldly ends, the tithes, talents, time, etc., which they, in the beginning, deeded unto God (Lev.27:28-34), or agreed to use as material for temple or character building. 

Then, as though theft were not disgraceful enough, God's curse is shared with the false-swearer, hypocrite or nominal-Christian (Eze.36:20; Acts 19:13-17; Phil. 

3:18), who endeavors to palm off a blank or unfinished negative for a genuine-portrait of the Christ. Just as a photographer may pretend to take a negative-likeness but, not even expose the plate to the light; he thinking that, what is done in the dark-chamber of his imagery, is hidden from his patron (Eze.8:12). The word declares that such deceptions are commonly practiced among Church members, everywhere. And the business

of the F. R. is to expose them; and to explain how they are calling down upon themselves the wrath-of-God. 

No doubt, but some people do these things ignorantly, and are more or less blameless. They shall, nevertheless suffer the consequences of the law, as ignorance excuses or licenses no man (Lev.5:17). And the light-of-the-F.R. makes them, henceforth, responsible for their sin. Punishment is sure to follow, unless they repent and do their first-works (Acts 17:30; Rom.8:9; Rev.2:5).

We, in the beginning, can only see the perfect-Christ, our Pattern, in his word; and as its prophecy is fulfilled in the lives of his Eveready saints (Phil.3:17). But at last, we may see him in ourselves, by perfecting his image (mind) and likeness (life). We must disregard the slanderous reports of the thieves and false-swearers, who have stolen or discounted the beauty or perfection of Christ's-holiness (Num.14:36; Phil.3:18-19; Heb.12: 

1-3). Truly, his face was "so marred, more than any man's;" and his form, more than the sons of men (Isa. 52:14).

False-swearers, who compell their consciences to agree to their lies (1 Tim.4:2); and take refuge in those lies (Psa.73:4-5; Isa.28:17), are antichrist-for they thus defeat the purpose of the law to make Christians out of them. Either hypnotic auto-suggestion, or projectionso called "Christian Science" (Col.2:8; 1 Tim.6:20)-is an abuse of will-power (1 Sam.15:23), in that it makes a false-prophet out of the conscience (Rev.20:10).

Surely condemnation-for-sins and sickness-of-body are easier to bear than the "curse of God" (Isa.5:20-24). 

No scales are "too accurate" for an honest man; but balance-tamperers revel in self-deception. A false-balance is an abomination to the Lord; but a just-weight is his delight (Pro.11:1). Confusion or violation-of-conscience is the Mark of the Beast-for it gives the flesh ascendency over the spirit. Regeneration accurately adjusts the balance - of- conscience, viz., body vs. spirit. 

Anything short of repentance does not do this; but is a delusion and false-sealing of the antichrist (Isa.28:15; 

Lk.13:3; 1 Jhn.2:19). 

A negative washed free of developer, or a conscience with premature-peace (Jer.6:14), without the fixingbath of "true repentance," will fade away in the searchlight of the "final judgment day" hereinafter described (Psa.1:5; Mat.22:11-13). Thieves and robbers

do gain admittance to the Church or Christian-sheepfold (Jhn.10:1); but the Shepherd will claim his own, and separate the goats from the sheep (Mat.25:23).

Confessing sin off of the conscience to Christ's adversary, the visible-head of the Church, who usurps his authority-even as the body lusts against the spirit, in the mind is blasphemy for both parties in the transaction (Psa.109:6; Eze.14:6-11; 22:25-31); for no man has authority to forgive sins, but God alone (Psa.130:4; 

Eze.28:6-10; Rom.14:12-13). When a man presumes to take the place of God in judgment, he becomes a "positive-enemy to character-building, a spiritual-abortionist."

The transgressor who accedes to antichrist doctrinethat sin is error or unreality, and should be deniedthat all is good and, therefore, there is no evil, devil, etc., in direct contradiction to scripture (Job 2:10; Isa. 

45:7; Amo.3:6). Or who accepts a counterfeit-pardon of sin, in substitution for the witness of the Holy Ghost -who alone, knows when a sinner has truly repentedis woefully swindled. These two "fruitless branches" of the true-vine (Jhn.15:1-8), typing the mental and physical divisions of the "abomination which desolates," professing to be parts of the Christian Church, blight the seed-of-faith sown in the sinner's heart. They are abominable, because they claim to be Christian, when they are actually antichrist. They are desolating, because they are spiritual-abortionists (Isa.66:9; Jer.6:14; 

Lk.13:3,5). Disappointment awaits those who anticipate any other fulfillment of this prophecy (Mat.24:15; Mk.13:14).

The sinner, who has "thus tampered with the scalesof-judgment," hopes or guesses that he is saved; but he does not possess the "witness of perfect-assurance" which the scripture promises the repentant, and which "others apparently have" (Jhn.1:12; 3:3,8; Acts 3:19; 

Rom.8:16). This accounts for the doubts and fears which fill the minds of many nominal-Christians (Acts 

8:21-23; Rom.14:23; Gal.3:13,25). 

However, repentance, real or imaginary, eliminates condemnation or the sense-of-guilt from the conscience; clears up the "darkness of fear;" and the law becomes void. The born-again Christian is now a new-creature. 

And the false-swearer thinks that he is too ... ..because he has cheated the balances and made lies his refuge

(2 Thes.2:8-12; 1 Jhn.1:8-10), and so thwarted the purpose-of-the-law to bring him to Christ. And his spirit shall be cut off, at death, from the body-as depicted by separating the black-sections of the F. R. from the cover. 

And "head downward"-note the position of these darkwings, when unfolded, in connection with the outsidepages of the cover, which type the body-the sinner shall be cast into outer-darkness, as an "unprofitable servant" (Mat.25:30; Rev.22:15).

When unfolded, the inside-pages of the F. R. type heaven; and the outside-pages type hell, of the sinner; and the "day of the Lord's vengeance," of the foolishvirgin (Lk. 21:22-23; Rev.22:15). The beginning and ending of God's law of character-building, operating in our members, is typed by the body-covers of the F. R. and, throughout eternity, they will be "mute reminders" of a profitable or misspent life, which was, or was not, taken captive (Isa.14:2; 30:8; 45:14; Rev.20:12). The front-cover representing-what life was; and the rearcover-what it might have been. 

A hypocrite fellowships with the elect, as being one of them; but his place as a Judas traitor-is under the law (Acts 1:25). The "sign of the prophet-Jonas" is intended for such, that they should confess themselves, in the belly of - leviathan, or spiritually-dead- after 

Christ's example-spending three days and nights in the tomb, until resurrected by the Holy Spirit or angel-of- 

God, into life or spirituality (Mat.12:39-40; Eph.2:1-7; Phil.3:20-21). 

The broken-pitchers of Gideon, which contributed toward the defeat of the Midianites (Judg.7:20; Job 41: 

18), were types of the leviathan-lamp, that new, sharp threshing-instrument, having teeth-explaining scripture, so as to produce biting-conviction (Isa.41:15,2123)-will be used by the Gideon Christians to drive out the antichrists who desecrate the holy Sanctuary. These 

Midianites, who will not humble themselves by going under-the-law or regard the Sign-of-the-prophet-Jonas, must be exterminated with the sword-of-the- Spirit (Eph.5:13-14; 6:17; Heb.4:12).

As grand as the justified Christian-experience is, it only represents the "foundation of character-building;" even as a photographer's negative only represents the foundation of portraits or image-building. And we, too, are now only half-way through the F. R.

The next thing to be considered in the "art of spiritual-photography," as relating to the science of character-building, is the "positive plate, film, or print," which is the "superstructure of photography"—as sanctification is of salvation. A positive-image is a reversal of the negative-image. The truth is told in the secondpicture, instead of a lie. No camera is required in this case: simply place a fresh plate, or piece of printingpaper, in direct contact with the negative. Expose them briefly in the sunlight, or before an electric-lamp, and you have a positive-picture. A "wrong impression" is thus corrected . . . truth is vindicated. The "lying negative" is now laid aside and, henceforth, the positive alone, is consulted for true-features. 

The same rule holds good in "spiritual photography," i.e., a justified-Christian is enlightened by being subjected to a fiery trial-of-his-faith (Deu.32:30; 32:2; Isa.14: 

26-32; 1 Pet.4:12)-even his flesh is destroyed with indulgence (1 Cor.5:5)—the carnal-mind is soon discovered to be "no-small handicap" (Rom.8:6-7; Heb.12:1); and gradually it becomes loathsome (Rom.7:24; Heb. 

12:15-17). One's only self-defense is to make a livingsacrifice of the lying or negative body: favoring conscience-directed reason, instead of sense (1 Thes.4:3-7).

But the "positive-image of Christ" must also be treated in the dark-room, with first the developer, and then in the fixing-bath or it, too, will fade away like an unfinished-negative, if brought to the light; but if kept constantly in the dark regarding the better-self, unfinished-images will last indefinitely. How much like procrastinating-sinners, who tarry long in the fearfuldarkness of Egypt, before repenting. And like foolishvirgins, who aimlessly wander, practically all their lives, in the darkness of wilderness rebellion or shame, before consecrating themselves unto God. And a large proportion of these are inexcusable, for they purposely shun holiness churches, saints, and literature . . . for fear that they shall be enlightened and condemned, or contaminated with the "doctrine of a clean-heart.' Ignorance, to them, is bliss; but they fail to consider the finalconsequences of their neglect (Heb.2:3). How much better to hang the "symbol of pay-day" upon the darkroom door; and joyfully welcome the terrors of the developing-fluid and the fixing-bath of "God's gracious-

law" (Rom.5:3-5; 8:18). Thereafter, the sunlight-of- 

Truth won't hurt the conscience a particle (Jhn.3:1921).

The difference between negative and positive photographs is no more pronounced, than the "contrast between carnal and spiritual minded Christians." Entire consecration-all on the altar-like the photographer's developer, brings the true and perfect image-of-Christ into view (Gen.1:18,21; Rom.8:29; Eph.4:13). The "ideallife for everybody" is portrayed in this second, fearless conception of Christ (1 Jhn.4:15-18). Sanctification, like the photographer's hypo-bath, stabilizes and prevents the Christ-image from back-sliding (1 Pet.5:10; 

2 Pet.2:20-22). 

Cessation of condemnation and shame, is like preventing the "further-action of the developing and fixative fluids upon the positive - portrait," by washing them off in pure water (1 Cor.6:11). And bringing the plates, prints, or films out into daylight for close-inspection. This act is symbolized by "flinging back” the darksections of the F. R., a second time. And is accompanied by a "sense of personal-relief”-not unlike what is experienced, after "resolutely discarding a depressing blanket," which has temporarily served the purpose of an imaginary-refuge, in time of fear or shame (Psa. 

139:7-12,18; Isa.30:22; Jhn.3:19-21). This exercise-offaith, simultaneously eliminates the old-man or fallennature from the mind-which is now single instead of dual-and the third-wing from the F. R., as it were, to symbolize the event.

As the photographer stands back of a "finished portrait," to display it; and not before it, as when his task of producing it has not yet begun-this types a Christian's "satisfaction of possessing both installments-ofholiness." The infolding of the F. R. wings, you will notice, takes the positive-plate or third-wing out of the dark-room; and replaces it in its proper-order, as secondary, or as superstructure to the "foundation-experience of regeneration," as typed by the second-wing.

Christian pentecost, or the witness of the Holy Ghost to sanctification, is thus symbolized by the F. R. 

There is no third-plate used in the art of photography; and there is no third-installment of salvation or character-building-prefigured by an additional-wing, above the third of the F. R. The light of noon-day or

singlemindedness now illuminates the "mystery of God's plan-of-character-building" (1 Cor. 13:12; Heb. 2:11; 5: 

14). We here see, exactly what was in sight at justification or the new-birth, only in another-light, or with a new pair-of-eyes, which is all the result of a "change of mind" (Rom.12:1-2). Pentecost, or crossing over from carnality to spirituality, makes possible a "burning testimony to a spiritual-mind" (1 Cor.2:9-16): as indicated by the three-lower wheels or lamps in the fourfold-group, on page-ten of the unfolded F. R., instead of just the lower one, which previously burned in "testimony to sins forgiven." The individual's personality and power-for-service are now trebled. His eye and love are single in the interests of God's kingdom; he has divorced the world and the flesh (Mat.6:22-24).

Leviathan is cast out of the heavenly-mind, and is made to serve in the body-of-earth (Rev.12:9; 20:1-3); a Holy Ghost directed conscientious-reason seals him up, or keeps him captive. Sensation, or lust of the eyes and the flesh, personified in the mind, will make a Satan out of any of us (Jas.1:13-15). Is salvation, explained, or without mystery, any "less-precious"?

The symbolism of pay-day, on the dark-room door, above the heads of the spiritual-creatures, is now threefourths fulfilled-prophecy or compensation; against onefourth, at conversion, when the negative-image of Christ was brought forth from the dark-room into the light-ofday; and stamped with the Holy Spirit's seal-of-acceptance (Rom.8:16). 

At this juncture, the "finishing process" only, remains to be completed. Which consists of drying, mounting, retouching or tinting, burnishing, and framing—all of which puts Christ's portrait in "its best-light," or makes it become our Example. And the more-surely will please him (Col.1:26-29; Rev.21:2). 

The second and third wings of the F. R., typing two changes-of-mind or the "funeral-services of the firstborn and the old-man," are now, as it were, after breaking the fifth-seal (Gen.1:20-23; Rev.6:9-11), cut away from the balance of the F. R. And we see Christ transfigured before us in majestic-holiness, or portrayed as the "Sun... shining in-his-splendor." For the promise is that, the righteous shall shine forth as the Sun in the kingdom of their Father (Mat.5:14-16; 13:43; Jhn.8: 

12). The law (Moses) and the prophets (Elias)-in
